    - *Left: Rust's float-to-integer casts saturate. x86-64 at its baseline (SSE2) has no instruction that does, so the compiler converts one lane at a time, about a tenth of the frame here.   - *An error is placed by whole characters. A letter typed where none goes, `é` or `🇺`, was an error one byte wide, inside the letter, and placing it in the page panicked.   - *Data far past 10^22 ticks as any other. The tick step's power of ten came from a table that stops at 10^22, so a value of 1e300 asked for 10^277 ticks. Past the table, the power is the literal as Rust reads it, correctly rounded on every platform, and a range no step divides has no ticks (`ticks_hold_at_the_ends_of_f64`).*
  - *A JSON data row lines up with its columns by name. A row that named its columns in another order put its values under the wrong ones, and a row that left one out panicked validation. - [x] 2.60 Versions (SPEC §8): a bundle that keeps a history lists its changes by author and time; one chosen shows the deck as it was, read only; two compared say what changed (`deck_diff`); and Restore makes a version the deck again, one change by `user`, which undoes as any other.
  *(Done: SPEC §7.1, §7.2, §8.2, §9.2, ADR-0013.*
  - *A version is the deck as it was just after one change the history keeps, with the data files its sources named then (`DeckDoc::at`, a fork at the change's last operation). Loro stores one large commit as several changes that continue each other, with the same author, message, and time: `DeckDoc::changes` joins them, so a version is a whole commit, named by its first operation's id.*
  - *`scaena history` lists the versions; `--at` gives one's deck (`--scn` as source); `--diff A[,B]` says what changed from one to another or to the deck now, state by state (each state's own fields, and its nodes resolved with the overrides, as `diff` says them), then the deck's own fields, the states' order, and the data files whose bytes differ; `--restore` makes one the deck again with its data files, one change (`history --restore <id>`), checked and refused as a patch is (`scaena_ops::history`). `deck_history` is the MCP tool (15 tools); the assistant has none, since its module keeps no CRDT.*
  - *The editor's Versions tab reads the history with the history's own module (`changes`, `at`), loaded the first time. A version chosen is drawn read only by a session of its own (`Player.viewVersion`, `versionPng`), or says why it cannot be (a file it names taken out since), beside what changed from it to the deck now or to another version (`Player.compareVersions`). Restore (`Player.restoreVersion`) writes as any operation's edit does (`Session::write`): kept for the save, the data file's bytes before kept as the bundle's own, so the save records the restore and nothing as changed outside Scaena. The source takes the deck as one CodeMirror change that carries the files written, before and after; its undo writes them back and its redo writes them again (`Player.writeFiles`).*
  - *Tests: `crates/scaena-store/tests/crdt.rs` (a commit's pieces are one change; a version's deck and data files; a version by an id inside it), `history_lists_shows_compares_and_restores_versions` (CLI), the MCP tool's schema and calls, `scaena-history`'s `at`, `a_version_is_shown_compared_restored_and_undone` (WASM session), and `web/versions.mjs` (`just web-smoke`, CI): the revenue example saved with a history and changed by `scaena` five times; the list; a version shown, another state of it, compared with the deck now and with two others; Restore, ⌘Z and ⇧⌘Z with the data file; the save that records it; a version whose image is gone, not drawn and refused, with why; and axe-core.)*
- [x] 2.61 The theme, edited (SPEC §3.6, an ADR first: a theme's edits as patches, and where a shipped theme's edits are kept): its colors, type roles, and spacing in a panel; each edit one patch of the theme the bundle holds, the deck laid out again, refused with why where the deck would not validate in it.
  *(Done: ADR-0016, SPEC §3.6, §7.1, §7.2, §8.1, §8.2, §9.2, ADR-0013.*
  - *A theme edit is RFC 6902 operations on the theme the deck names, checked as a re-theme is: refused where the deck would not validate in the theme it leaves (a name it uses taken out, E102; a value the schema refuses, E106), and otherwise written in canonical form, as `save` writes it, with the lint delta. An inline theme's are the same operations under `/theme`, written into `deck.json`. A theme that ships is edited in the bundle's copy; the picker puts the one that ships beside an edited copy (`dusk-2.theme.json`), never over it.*
  - *The history keeps the theme the deck names beside its data files (`scaena_store::kept_paths`): an edit is one change with the theme's bytes (`theme_edit: tokens/color/paper`), a save records the theme as it writes it, and a theme changed on disk goes in as a change by `fs`, once for a history from before. A version is the deck, its data files, and its theme; comparing one held before the history kept the theme says nothing of it (`differing` skips bytes a side does not know).*
  - *`scaena theme BUNDLE --edit ops.json` (or `-`), and the MCP tool `theme_edit` (16 tools), on `scaena_ops::theme::theme_edit` and its twin that writes nothing (`theme_editing`).*
  - *The editor's Theme tab, beside Data, shows the theme frames are drawn in (`Player.themeText`): its colors, each with the color roles that name it, its type roles, and its spacing. Each change is one `theme_edit` by `user` (`Player.themeEdit`). W427 finds a figure a file changed behind the deck's back, and the claim with it, each with its fix (`tests/lint/W427/`). A quote's source the deck lacks is E102; its row, column, or empty cell E103; its format or transform E106. `style_text` takes `quote`; a figure is styled whole, typing into it makes it words, and typing beside it goes in a run of its own. In the editor, the Data tab's Quote quotes the cell focused in the characters selected, its row by the columns of text that tell it apart (`web/quotes.mjs`, 10 checks). The site's pages register it (`web/src/offline.ts`, only where `VITE_BUNDLE` says this is the site). It keeps every file as it installs, so the engine and each module loaded later (the subsetter, the history, the PDF writer, what the assistant reads, the hyphenation patterns, the themes) come from the cache. It answers a deck's files from the network and keeps them for when there is none, and lets other origins and `scaena serve`'s events pass. Its cache is named for the files' contents, so a new build takes over once no page uses the last. The pages say "Works offline". `web/offline.mjs` opens the site once, takes the network away, then plays every state of the demo deck, edits and lints it, and downloads it (15 checks). The editor's module does not change; the site gains `sw.js`, `manifest.webmanifest`, and `icon.svg`.*

- [x] 2.82 A contrast error offers a fix: the theme's color that reads against what lies behind the text (Jay, 2026-10-07: as the 2.77 walk recommended).
    - Done. A text node's E110 or E111 carries the patch that chooses it, as the inspector chooses a color, the theme's color whose worst reading is best: a `choose` of `style/color` at the state it reads worst in, written where its color lives (`scaena_engine::lint::contrast::recolor`, after every format is linted). Lint keeps it only where the text reads in it in every state and format the choice reaches. Text is taken away from what lies behind any text, so the pixels painted once judge each color. First, each color is screened over what lies under the runs that failed, as lint's own reading kept it, without sorting (`reads`, held to `read`'s verdict by `reads_is_read_at_the_bar`): only a color that reads there is judged where the choice reaches, which lays states out and paints them. The bench gate caught the version without the screen at +106% on `lint_layout/b4`, the torture deck, whose `case` over the mesh reads in no color; with it, lint on the torture deck takes what it did, within the noise of five runs each. A probe color the text has nowhere tells the runs that take the node's color from those `style_text` gave their own. `scaena_core::patch::compile_alone` compiles the choice without setting quoted figures again, so a fix changes no text. The trails deck re-themed in Daybreak sets its storm slide's title and note dark on the dark photo (E111 at 1.09:1, E110 at 1.07:1); each fix sets `surface`, and `lint --fix` leaves no contrast error. The torture deck's `case` over the mesh gets none: no color of the theme reads over the whole gradient, at rest and at the end of its hold. A chart's text and a table's are set in the theme's roles for them, and get no fix. `a_contrast_fix_chooses_the_color_that_reads_wherever_the_choice_reaches` holds the fixture's fix, its `lint --fix`, no fix where the choice reaches a light panel and the dark surface both, and one written in a state's own delta where the color lives there. The editor's mark says an object set whole by its keys (`color: surface`), and `web/edit-deck.mjs` takes each fix on the storm slide in Daybreak in one click, sees each error go, and undoes both. The editor's engine module is 2,867.93 kB gzipped, as Vite reports it: 9.4 kB more than after 2.79, the most any Phase 2 task has added, which leaves 132 kB under SPEC §15's 3 MB.
    - *On Jay's call (2026-10-13), a re-theme takes the fix itself. Trails re-themed in Daybreak had left its storm slide's words dark on the dark photo, each fix a click away on its mark. Now each text a theme would leave too faint to read where it stands, which E110 or E111 finds with a fix, is set in the theme's color that reads there in the same change, so a deck reads in a theme it takes as it read in its own (`scaena_ops::theme::theming`, its `recolored`, one finding for each text). Nothing else in the deck changes, and a theme edit recolors nothing, since its colors are the edit. `theme --apply` says each text it recolored, `theme_apply` returns them, and the browser's status, the Mac's, and the iPad's say how many. `a_text_the_theme_leaves_too_faint_is_set_in_its_color_that_reads` holds trails in Daybreak: its storm title and note set in a color of the theme, no contrast error, and nothing else changed. Every example re-themed in every theme leaves no contrast error its fix would mend, and the session's `a_theme_chosen_in_the_editor_recolors_what_it_would_leave_too_faint` holds the editor's re-theme to the same.*

    - Done. Trails could not be laid out well in 9:16 by slots alone. Its process slide is a grid of four steps across, and a container's tracks were the same in every format. So a node may now say how it lays out in each of the deck's formats (ADR-0020, deck format 0.13, SPEC §3.4):
      - `formats: {"9:16": {…}}` sets its `at`, `size`, `align`, and `transform` there, a container's `axis`, `gap`, `distribute`, `padding`, `cols`, `rows`, and `areas`, and a text's `maxLines`;
      - `scaena_engine::project` puts each in place of the node's own when it lays the deck out in that format;

      - a node placed anew in each format is not W302.

      In 9:16, trails stacks its process steps with an arrow down between each, puts the change slide's before over its after, and gives the budget table the full width. Its titles keep their cells, and its quote takes a fourth line.

      Its plan line's label said "Plan: 6.7 miles a month". In 9:16 the bars stand too close for that to sit clear of them (E111), so it says "Plan: 6.7" in both formats: the caption gives the units. A rule's label now also moves along the rule, past the marks that rise through it, where its start is not clear (`charts::compile`).

- [x] 2.85 The editor places a node in the format shown: where the node lays out anew there, a drag, a resize, or an arrow key moves it there alone.
    - Done (ADR-0020). `place` takes `format` and `anew` (SPEC §7.3):
      - with `format`, a node that has its own layout there (`formats`) is placed in that layout's `at`, and its own `at` stays, so the deck's own canvas is as it was;
      - a node with none is placed as before, in its own `at`, in every format;
      - `anew` gives it a layout of its own there, placed so; refused in a format the deck does not lay out anew, and `fork` is refused with `format` where the layout is the node's own.
    - The engine's targets carry the format they were asked in (`Targets.format`), so every gesture's `place` ops and `inspect --snap`'s say it, and the editor's patches with them.
    - The editor's menus offer "Place anew in this format" for a node with no layout of its own in the format shown; it copies the node's placement there.

    - Done (ADR-0020). Probing what 2.84 and 2.85 left beside them found two faults:

Now `Session::duplicating` places it in each format the node lays out in: the one shown, the deck's own canvas, and each with a layout of its own there, each clear of the rest where there is room. In trails, `budget-why` duplicated in 9:16 went beside itself there and onto itself in 16:9; now it stands clear in both.
      - **A paste kept the placements the clip had in its formats.** Pasted into its own deck in 9:16, the copy landed on the node it came from; pasted into a deck that lists no 9:16, it brought a layout the deck refuses. Now a pasted node keeps a layout only for a format the deck lays out anew, less its placement there, so it goes where it is pasted in each (`scaena_ops::inspect::copied_layouts`). A node held by a container is held by the container's copy in its formats' layouts too.
    - `Deck::anew` names the formats a deck lays out anew, for W302 and the copies.

    - Done. Probing the editor's design tools found that a deck made or opened in the browser could never keep a history: the Versions tab said to run `scaena save --history`, which a person on the site cannot do. Now, where the bundle keeps none, the tab offers **Keep a history**. It saves the bundle where it is kept, or into the browser's storage, and that save begins the history (`Player.keepHistory`, `Session::save`): the deck as saved and the files it is drawn from are its first version, `user`'s `history begins`, as the CLI's is, and each save after records the edits since.

- [x] 2.91 Room in the editor's module for the chart forms (1.29–1.31): it was 2,891 kB gzipped by Vite, 109 kB under SPEC §15's 3 MB, and each new chart form adds to it (the parent session, 2026-10-07).

        - Layout's crates build for size in the WASM profile: taffy, parley, harfrust, fontique, and the icu crates whose tables they read. They run as a deck is laid out, never as a frame samples it (SPEC §5): 68 KB of the cut, by `gzip -9`.

The chart compiler's were left for the chart forms' own pass. After 1.31, it and three others build their sets so too, and `crates/scaena-wasm/tests/sorts.rs` finds a `collect` typed as a sorted map or set.
    - Timed in headless Chromium, with the CPU painter and no helper workers, on the trails deck:
        - Without a shader, a state paints in 7.69 ms on average. The two runs before the change read 8.12 and 7.96 ms.
        - The shader states paint as before: cover in 42.1 ms against 42.3, storm in 24.0 against 26.9, and next in 229.6 against 244.8.
        - B1 starts cold in 217 ms, against 218 before (the median of 9 runs).
    - Measured and not taken:
        - `scaena-core` at `z` is 252 KB smaller, but the shader states' CPU references paint 3.4–4× slower: cover in 143 ms, next in 1,012 ms.
        - `scaena-engine` at `z` is 156 KB smaller, but every frame without a shader paints 7% slower: 8.6 ms against 8.0, in two runs of each.
        - Neither is worth it while gate 2's first criterion, frames a second in a real machine's browsers, is still to be read.
        - `SemanticOp`'s deserializer, at 117 KB the largest function, is an internally tagged enum: serde buffers each op whichever deserializer reads it, so it stays as it is.
- [x] 2.92 Suggested layouts: the inspector draws the state shown in each layout it may take, judged by lint, best first, and one click gives it that layout (the parent session, 2026-10-07, at Jay's "ever forward").
    - Done. `scaena_ops::layouts` takes each layout `state_choices` offers the state, makes its `set_state`, written where the layout lives, lints the state in every format the deck lists, and draws it at rest. The layouts come fewest errors first, then fewest warnings; of those alike, the one the state takes now, then the theme's order. Each carries its patch and the states it changes.
    - A suggestion draws the state otherwise, or it is none. A layout drawn as one before it, with the same counts, is that one's `alike`: on trails' budget slide, `poster` and `full` set its note where `figure` does, so the slide is offered `figure` and `narrow-figure`. A state that places no node in a slot is offered none: a layout is its slots.
    - `scaena inspect --state S --layouts` lists them, and the MCP server's `deck_inspect` gives them with `layouts`, the assistant's too.
    - In the editor, with nothing selected, they stand above the state's fields (`Player.layoutSuggestions`), where there are two or more. Each is drawn at rest in the format shown, 72 pixels high, by the CPU painter in the worker, and says what lint finds there and which layouts draw it alike; the one the state takes now is pressed.
        - A pointer over one, or the focus on it, shows the state laid out in it on the canvas, nothing made (`Player.preview`), until the pointer or the focus leaves.
        - A click or Enter chooses it as the layout field does: one `set_state` by `user`, kept to the state with "layout only in", one step to undo.
        - They are judged again once edits pause, after the lint of every state, and when another state shows: typing never starts a round.
        - A round is the worker's longest task, so it judges a layout at a time (`Player.layoutsBegin`, `layoutsStep`) and answers what else the page asks between them; an edit meanwhile ends it. In headless Chromium on 4 cores, a round on the trails and revenue examples takes 80–430 ms, about 25–100 ms a layout. B1's edit round trip stays at a 28 ms median.
    - Tests: `crates/scaena-ops/tests/layouts.rs` (trails' budget slide offered figure, which poster and full draw alike, then narrow-figure in errors; storm a clean narrow-figure; the agenda one layout that every other draws alike; the change slide, its cards on the grid, none; `inspect` names the state), the WASM session's `a_states_layouts_come_judged_and_painted_best_first` (the CLI's list, painted, no two alike), and `web/suggest.mjs` (28 checks).
- [x] 2.93 What each of the assistant's edits changed, drawn: under each call that changed what a state draws, the state before and after, so the user sees each step the model took without looking for it (the parent session, 2026-10-07, at Jay's "ever forward").
    - Done. The page sends the strip's pictures' digests with the question (`Drawn`). As each edit is made, the worker draws each state whose drawing now differs at rest, as the strip draws it, before the assistant's next call can change it again, and sends the pictures with the edit. Each later edit is drawn against the one before.
    - The Assistant tab shows under the call each state before and after: the first four, how many more, and the states it took away. A state it added shows as new, and a click shows the state. Before the question goes, the strip draws what changed since it last did, so a user's own edit is not shown as the assistant's.

- [x] 2.94 A theme's colors from a photo: the photo's hues on the theme's own tones, each color text is set in kept where it reads (the parent session, 2026-10-07, at Jay's "ever forward").
    - Done. `scaena_core::palette` reads a photo in Oklab: its hues, best first, each scored by how much of the photo lies near it and how colorful it is there, and the hue all of it leans to. It gives a theme's colors those hues on the theme's own tones:
        - The accent takes the best hue, and the next chromatic color the best at least 60° from it. Each other chromatic color turns with the accent. Each keeps its lightness and chroma.
        - The neutrals take the photo's lean, a little of it.
        - A color text is set in moves the least step that reads on the surfaces at WCAG's ratio, as lint judges it.
        - The data palettes follow the theme's colors.

    - On the trails example, the ridge photo turns the accent from #FF6A3D to #B97CFF (6.3:1 on the surfaces) and accent-2 from #FFC857 to #FFBFBA, and leans the neutrals violet: ink reads 15.7:1, muted 5.2:1. Lint finds nothing new.

    - **A picture** joins the bundle and is inserted where the canvas was last pressed, as one dropped there is: a screenshot, a picture copied in a browser, or a file copied where files are kept, whose words only name it. One of a kind an image does not show, a GIF, a WebP, an AVIF, or a TIFF in Safari, joins as a PNG of it, dropped or pasted.

Any other column is text, even where its cells read as numbers, as `007` does.
    - **Text outranks a picture.** Excel copies a picture of the cells beside their text, and Keynote one of its words; the text pastes.
    - **Room for its columns.** A table, pasted or inserted, takes the grid's width where the half of the canvas Insert offers is too narrow for its columns in a format the deck lists. Before, the revenue example refused a five-column table: it fit 16:9, but not 9:16.
    - *A picture too large for a slide, past 8192 px a side, was refused with the engine's own words, "data: assets/….jpg: 11656 × 8742 px; images are at most 8192 px a side (SPEC §3.3)", and the file stayed in the bundle, which Insert then offered and the deck refused (Jay, 2026-10-12). Every way a picture comes in, a drop, a paste, Choose Picture…, or a drop on a picture, now reads its size first and asks whether a copy made to fit goes in, its longer side 8192 px, upright: a PNG stays a PNG, its transparency kept, and a JPEG a JPEG (ImageIO on the Mac and the iPad, a canvas in the browser), the original left as it is. A picture an image could not show stays out of the bundle. A JPEG's header now says its size whatever it is, nothing made for its pixels.*

- [x] 2.97 The light table: every slide in a grid, to move, copy, and take out across a large deck, each state showing what it showed (the parent session, 2026-10-08, at Jay's "ever forward").
    - Done. Slides shows the deck's slides in place of the preview, each at its last state, at rest, with its number, its id, its steps, and its findings (`web/src/slides.ts`). Selection is a file browser's: click, Shift+click, ⌘-click, the arrow keys, Space. Enter shows a slide in the canvas, and Escape closes the table.
    - **Every state shows what it showed.** States track from the state before them (SPEC §2.2), so moving a slide as `move_state` moves a state would change what the slide shows, and what the slide after it shows. Three new ops keep each state's look: `move_slide`, `duplicate_slide`, and `remove_slide` (SPEC §7.3). A state whose state before it changes has its delta written again, from the snapshots, to resolve as it did (`scaena_core::tracking::delta_to`, `keep_looks`). The deck is resolved after to prove it, and an order that cannot keep a look is refused with the state it would change.
    - **A copy is its own.** `duplicate_slide` makes the copy's first state absolute, setting every property of each node it shows, so an edit to either slide leaves the other; the copy shows the same nodes, so the slide morphs into it.
    - In the table: a drag, or Alt with an arrow, moves the slides selected; ⌘D copies them, the copies selected; Delete takes them out. Each is one patch, one step to undo. A slide's slot in the timeline now names its slide (`Player.timeline`), and the worker paints some states' thumbnails apart from the strip's.
    - Tests: `scaena_core::tracking`'s `a_delta_between_two_snapshots_resolves_as_the_second` and `states_moved_keep_their_looks`; the patch suite's `slides_move_copy_and_go_keeping_their_looks`; and `web/slides.mjs` on the revenue example, each state's reading compared before and after.
- [x] 2.98 Room under SPEC §15's 3.0 MB: core built for size, what is done pixel by pixel kept fast (the parent session, 2026-10-08, at Jay's "ever forward").
    - Done. Each shader kind's CPU reference and WGSL twin, and the JPEG decoder, move to a crate of their own, `scaena-pixels`, which core re-exports (`scaena_core::shader`, `scaena_core::jpeg`), so no caller changes. The `wasm` profile builds core at opt-level `z` and `scaena-pixels` at 3.
    - **The editor's module** is 2,595,394 bytes gzipped, from 2,850,052: 255 KB less, 405 KB under the budget where 2.97 left 150 KB. The player's, which a single file carries, is 2,126,786, from 2,179,408.
    - **What it costs.** In headless Chromium, on B1, compiling the source takes 9.6 ms, from 6.3, and linting the state edited 12.5 ms, from 8.7: a bit under half again, as typing goes. The editor's round trip is 35 ms, as gate 2 recorded it (`web/editor.mjs`), and linting every state 61 ms. Core at `s` saves 100 KB less for the same cost.
    - **What it keeps.** Shaders paint as fast: B3's states, 58–60 ms each, paint alike in both builds. Core alone at `s`, its shaders in it, paints B3 three times slower, 1,250 ms against 421. A 12-megapixel JPEG decodes in the same 200 ms, where core at `z` would take 35% longer. B1's paints, its frames, and its cold start are as they were.
    - Tests: every test moves with its code. `just wasm-smoke` and `just web-smoke` hold every torture state to the goldens, natively and in the browser, by WebGPU and the CPU painter; the `sorts` test walks `scaena-pixels` too; and CI's macOS runner takes a change to it as it takes one to the painters.

None of it reaches the MCP tools or the CLI, whose schemas are as they were.

The inspector labels each property, says each value, where it is set ("Set on this slide"), and how far a change reaches ("on 2 steps of Slide 2"). A finding is a sentence with its fix by name, and the status counts "1 problem, 2 warnings". An id is renamed only in the source. The worker keeps a state's words while only reads come, so an edit computes them once for the inspector, the layers, and the canvas.
    - **Every line the window says.** The status line says each gesture in these words, on the canvas and in a text typed in: an object by its words ("“Revenue doubled” → in the Title slot · on 2 steps of Slide 2"), a slot and a layout by the theme's names, what Alt keeps to this slide, and a refusal as each finding's sentence (`Lexicon.issues`). The Insert menu names each kind and each choice (`Lexicon.types`); the theme picker, each theme by its name, in this deck or built in; the Theme tab, each color and text style by the theme's names; the Data tab, a column's type in words and its file by name; the Files tab, what uses each file; the find bar, where each match is; and the Versions tab, each change in words, by whom, each of a version's slides by its number (`Player.versionSlides`), and what changed slide by slide. The assistant says each call by what it does (the Mac's `ToolWords`), its result counted, never by lint's codes, as `scaena_chat::tools::summary` says it for the Mac. ⌘K links to a slide by its number, a PNG exported is named by its slide, and the keys sheet, the palette, and the dialogs say "slide" and "object".

Its headlines, display, figures, quotes (italic), numerals, and hero numbers are set in Instrument Serif, a condensed serif of one weight, large and tight. The roles set in Inter keep Daybreak's sizes, on its baseline grid. The trails deck re-themed into it lints clean.
    - **Ships everywhere** a theme ships: `scaena new --theme gallery`, `deck_create`, the editor's New and theme picker, and the Mac's and iPad's (`scaena_ops::shipped`, `web/src/themes.ts`). Instrument Serif and its italic are subset by `scripts/build_bundle_fonts.py` from the pinned google/fonts commit, each sha256-pinned, with its license (OFL 1.1). `theme --apply` brings a theme's fonts the bundle lacks from beside the theme file, its own bundle's, so a deck in Dusk's families takes Instrument Serif with Gallery.

- [x] 2.103 A slide's number and footer, the theme's to place and the deck's to say (the parent session, 2026-10-13, at Jay's "the power of PowerPoint, the beauty and elegance of Keynote"; ADR-0028).
    - Done. **The deck says what shows** (deck format 0.19): `furniture: { number, footer }`, each slide's number and the words at its foot. A slide's first state may say `furniture: false` to show neither, or `true` to show them on a layout the theme leaves them off; a later step that says either is E106. The number is the slide's place in the deck, every slide counted, so a move, a copy, or a removal changes no byte and every number is right after it (`scaena_core::furniture`).
    - **The theme places them** (theme format 0.14): `furniture: { number, footer, except }`, each a line of words in a role across the grid's columns, in the margin at the foot or the head, aligned and colored, the number written by a `pattern` (`{n}`, `{total}`). `except` names the layouts that show neither. The shipped themes set the footer from the left across eight columns and the number at the right of the last, in `label`, and leave them off their titles, dividers, and pictures that bleed to the foot (`scripts/build_layouts.py`). A role, a color, a layout, or a column the theme lacks is E102. An older copy of a theme that ships takes the placement as an editor opens it, as it takes the layout library (`scaena_ops::theme::library`), so a deck made before can number its slides.
    - **The engine draws them** with each snapshot, over the slide's nodes (`Scene::furniture`), the same through a slide's steps, fading from one slide's to the next over the cue's transition. They are no nodes: nothing selects, hits, lists, or reads them. The PDF tags them as pagination artifacts. Lint judges them as text (E110, E111, E120), and W303 finds a node that draws where they stand.
    - **Set in a person's words**: `furnish` sets the deck's, and `set_state`'s `furniture` a slide's, on its first state; the inspector offers both with nothing selected (`StateChoices.furniture`), in the browser and in the Mac's and iPad's Format tab. `.scn` writes them as the header's `furniture:{…}` and a state's `furniture:false`.

- [x] 2.105 The presenter's keys, as a presentation app's: in the browser's player and presenter view, on the Mac, and on the iPad (the parent session, 2026-10-13, at Jay's "the power of PowerPoint, the beauty and elegance of Keynote").
    - Done. **A black or a white screen.** B or . turns the screen black, and W or , white, as PowerPoint's and Keynote's keys do. The slide is out of sight and the deck waits where it was: a cue is held where it stood, and no hold runs. A key or a click brings the slide back and does nothing more. The same key turns the screen back; the other key turns it the other color.
    - **A slide by its number.** A slide's number typed shows at the foot of the view, and Return goes there, its first state's cue played. Backspace (Delete on the Mac) takes a digit back, and Escape, or four seconds with no key, lets the number go. A number the deck has no slide for is said: "No slide 9: the deck has 3 slides". Typed in the presenter view, it is said there, not to the room.
    - **The laser.** L shows a red dot where the pointer is over the slide, and hides the pointer there. From the presenter view, its pointer over the slide it shows points the laser on the room's slide, and both views draw it. On the iPad, a touch held on the slide is the laser while it stays down, as Keynote's is, and it turns no slide.
    - **The keys, listed.** ? shows them, and ? or Escape puts them away.
    - **Where:**
        - the browser's player, its presenter view, and a single file's player (`web/src/player.ts`), the presenter view steering the player over the BroadcastChannel (`black`, `white`, `slide`, `laser`, `point`; the player answers `pointed`);
        - the Mac's and the iPad's stage and presenter's view (`Presenting.swift`, ScaenaKit's `Show`: `blanking`, `unblank`, `type`, `goTyped`). VoiceOver hears "3 of 12, black screen".
    - Tests:

- [x] 2.108 Words in a shape: a shape holds words, as a presentation app's does, which it places and which go where it goes (the parent session, 2026-10-13, at Jay's "the power of PowerPoint, the beauty and elegance of Keynote"; PowerPoint's and Keynote's text in a shape).
    - Done. **A text whose `at.parent` names a shape is its words** (deck format 0.21, SPEC §3.4): any shape but a line or an arrow. A shape holds one text and places it, so its words take no other key of `at`; a second text, a node that is not a text, or another key of `at` is E106, which says which (`validate::words_in`).
    - **Their box is the room inside the shape** (`scaena_engine::shapes::words_box`), less a pad of `space.4`, at most a twelfth of its shorter side: a `rect` less what its rounded corners take, the rectangle inscribed in an `ellipse`, and in a polygon or a path the largest box near the shape's proportions that stands inside its outline (`inscribed`), so a star's words stand in its middle and a callout's in its balloon. Unless they say otherwise, they stand in the middle both ways and shrink to fit, off the baseline grid, and W220 counts them among no paragraphs.
    - **They go where the shape goes.** Tracking brings them in wherever the shape enters and takes them out wherever it leaves; a `remove` of them alone keeps them out until the shape enters again (`tracking::words_of`). The shape paints under them, as a container does, and the layers list them under it.
    - **Typing in a shape.** A double click on a shape types in its words, in the browser, on the Mac, and on the iPad; from the keys, Enter goes into the words a shape holds as into a container, and on a shape that holds none, Alt+Enter (Option-Return) gives it words to type in. Where it holds none, it is given them first: a text in the body role, in the color the theme names to read on its fill (`onAccent` on `accent`), one `add_node` and one step to undo (`scaena_ops::inspect::wording`, `Session::wording`, `Player.wording`, the C ABI's `wording`, `ScaenaSession.wording`). The canvas reaches the words as the shape: a click on them selects the shape, as does typing in them stopped, and a drag moves it with them (`boxes`' `takesWords` and `words`).

- [x] 2.109 Connectors: a line or an arrow that joins two objects and follows them, as a presentation app's connector does (the parent session, 2026-10-13, at Jay's "the power of PowerPoint, the beauty and elegance of Keynote"; PowerPoint's and Keynote's connection lines, with the shape library of 2.106 and the words of 2.108 to draw diagrams).
    - Done. **A line or an arrow whose `connect` names two nodes joins them** (deck format 0.22, SPEC §3.4): `connect: { from, to, route? }`, the arrow pointing at `to`. What it joins places it, so it takes no `at` and no `points` of its own; each end is a node of the deck with a box of its own, not a group, another connector, or itself, and a `connect` on any other shape is E106, each saying which (`validate::connectors`; E102 for an end the deck lacks).
    - **It runs between them as each state lays them out** (`scaena_engine::shapes::Joint`): `straight` along the line between the boxes' middles, from one box's edge to the other's, or `elbow`, out of the side that faces the other box, across or down, turning twice halfway. Each end stands off its box by `space.2`, so an arrowhead points at a box. Its box is its route's bounds and its points its route's (`containers::Placement::joined`).
    - **It goes where they go.** Tracking takes it away wherever either end is gone (`tracking::connects_of`), and `remove_node` takes the connectors of what it removes with it. Between states it morphs point by point with them, and a drag of either end redraws it from where that end is drawn, nothing laid out (`Scene::moved`, `Scene::rerouted`). Its ends are its own in every state: a state or the overrides that set `connect` are E104.
    - **Connecting, in the browser, on the Mac, and on the iPad.** With two objects selected, Connect with an arrow, or with a line, joins the first to the second: from the command palette and a right click in the browser, and the Arrange menu and the canvas's right click or long press on the Mac and the iPad. It is one `add_node` that enters in the state shown and stays on its slide, one step to undo, the connector selected (`scaena_ops::inspect::connecting`, `Session::connecting`, `Player.connecting`, the C ABI's `connecting`, `ScaenaSession.connecting`). A pointer picks a connector by its line, not its mostly empty box (`Scene::route`, `boxes`' `joins` and `route`, `NodeBox.holds`). It has no handles; a drag or an arrow key on it says to move either end, an alignment or a spread passes it by, and ⌘D refuses it. Copied with what it joins, its copy joins their copies (`scaena_ops::clipboard::pasting`); copied alone, it pastes as a line.
    - Tests: core's `a_connector_joins_two_nodes_of_their_own_and_is_placed_by_them` and `a_connector_leaves_wherever_either_end_does`; the engine's `a_connector_runs_between_what_it_joins_wherever_each_is_placed`; ops' `two_nodes_are_joined_by_a_connector_that_goes_where_they_go`; the session's `a_connector_is_picked_by_its_line_and_follows_a_drag`, which also copies and pastes one with its ends and alone, refuses ⌘D, aligns past one, and deletes an end with it; the C ABI's `two_nodes_are_connected_as_the_browser_connects_them`; ScaenaKit's `twoNodesAreJoinedByAnArrowPickedByItsLine`; and the walk `connect`: two objects joined by an arrow from the palette, the arrow picked by its line, a drag of one end redrawing it, the drop laying it out between them, and each step undone.
- [x] 2.110 Diagrams from a list: a text's lines drawn as a process, a cycle, or a hierarchy of boxes with words, joined by connectors, as a presentation app's SmartArt draws a list (the parent session, 2026-10-13, at Jay's "the power of PowerPoint"; PowerPoint's Convert to SmartArt, from the shape library of 2.106, the words of 2.108, and the connectors of 2.109).
    - Done. **A text selected is drawn as a diagram in its place** (`scaena_ops::diagrams::diagramming`): one patch that adds a container placed as the text was, in each format too, holding a box for each step with its words, joined by connectors, each entering in the state shown and kept to its slide, then deletes the text from the state on, as Delete does. Nothing new in the format: the diagram is the deck's own nodes, so every editor moves, restyles, and types in them as any other, and the container lays them out again wherever the text's place goes.
    - **Its steps are the text's lines** that hold words, at their list levels. A **process** is a stack of rounded boxes joined by arrows, across where the place is wider than tall, each as tall as four thirds of its width where there is room, and down where it is taller; a **cycle** is a grid of 24 tracks each way, its boxes on a ring that fills it, the first at the top, the largest that keep a track clear of each other, each joined to the next by an arrow and the last to the first; each takes the shallowest lines as its steps, a deeper line joining the box above it as a line of its own. A **hierarchy** is a grid a column for each box at the tree's foot and a row for each level, each box over its own's columns, one column wide and centered there, the top ones in the accent and the rest on `surface-2`, each joined to the one it is under by a line that turns at right angles. The boxes take the theme's names: its accent (or first color), `radius.3`, `space.7` between them, their words in `body`, centered, shrunk to fit, in the color that reads on the fill, and the connectors the `regular` stroke in `onSurfaceMuted`.
    - **Refused, with why**: a node that is no text or is a shape's words, one line, a cycle of two, more than eight steps, a hierarchy of more than 24 boxes, more than eight side by side, or more than five levels deep, and a hierarchy with no line under another, which says to indent them with Tab.
    - **An elbow goes down between boxes one above the other** (`Joint::route`), even where one stands to the side too, as an organization chart's lines run, and across where they stand side by side.
    - **Browser, Mac, and iPad.** The command palette's and a text's right click's Make a process diagram, Make a cycle diagram, and Make a hierarchy diagram; on the Mac and the iPad, Insert › Diagram from Text and the canvas's right click or long press. One step to undo, the diagram selected (`Session::diagramming`, `Player.diagramming`, the C ABI's `diagramming`, `ScaenaSession.diagramming`).
    - Tests: ops' `a_texts_lines_are_drawn_as_a_diagram_in_its_place` (each kind's patch, the text gone, it validating and kept to its slide, each refusal), `a_cycles_boxes_stand_on_a_ring_clear_of_each_other`, and `lines_make_steps_and_a_tree_by_their_levels`; the engine's connector test, an elbow down to a box below and to one side, and across to one beside; the session's `a_texts_lines_are_drawn_as_a_diagram_whose_boxes_hold_their_words`; the C ABI's `a_texts_lines_are_drawn_as_a_diagram_as_the_browser_draws_them`; ScaenaKit's `aTextsLinesAreDrawnAsADiagramInItsPlace`; and the walk `diagram`: a subtitle typed as three lines, drawn as a process from the palette and as a cycle from a right click, each one step to undo, and a hierarchy refused for lines at one level.
- [x] 2.111 Slide transitions as a presentation app's: Dissolve, Push, Move In, Reveal, and Wipe, each in a direction (the parent session, 2026-10-13, at Jay's "the beauty and elegance of Keynote"; Keynote's and PowerPoint's slide transitions beside Scaena's Magic Move).
    - Done. **A transition's `style` makes it a slide transition** (deck format 0.23, SPEC §3.9): `dissolve`, `push`, `cover`, `reveal`, or `wipe`, and its `direction`, `left` (the default), `right`, `up`, or `down`, the way the slides move. The state before is drawn whole, at rest, and the state entered whole, as at the transition's end with its own motions running on; nothing matches across them, so a node both show is drawn in each. Each is a layer with the canvas's ground, which the style moves, uncovers, fades, or clips over the transition's duration and curve (`scaena_engine::sample::Slide`), a spring stopping where it ends rather than overshooting. They are the display list's own layers, so every painter, the player, the Mac and the iPad, and the video export draw it with nothing new. The state before's exit motions do not run, and lint W322 says so of one written there.
    - **The inspector offers it** with no node selected, as it offers the rest of the transition (`scaena_core::choices::state_choices`): Transition (Dissolve, Push, Move In, Reveal, Wipe) and Direction (Right to Left, Left to Right, Bottom to Top, Top to Bottom), each one `set_state`, in the browser, on the Mac, and on the iPad, in plain words (`scaena_core::words`, `Words.swift`); the browser's cue listing names it, as `inspect --timeline` reports it (`style`, `direction`).
    - Tests: the engine's `a_slide_transition_moves_each_slide_whole`, each style a quarter of the way through: the two layers' offsets, a dissolve's opacity, a wipe's clip, each slide holding its own nodes, and the states before and after at rest exactly; core's choices offering the two fields; W322's fixtures, an exit under a slide transition flagged and an entrance on a node both slides show not; and the walk `transition`: the state inspector's Transition and Direction chosen, each one patch and one step of undo, the cue's listing naming it.
- [x] 2.112 Builds as a presentation app's: the shipped themes offer Keynote's and PowerPoint's most used build effects as motion presets, each named as those apps name it (the parent session, 2026-10-13, at Jay's "the beauty and elegance of Keynote"; Keynote's Build In and Build Out, PowerPoint's Animations).
    - Done. **Dusk, Daybreak, Ember, and Gallery each offer 24 presets** (SPEC §3.6): to enter, Appear (at once), Fade, Rise, Word by Word, Typewriter (letter by letter, each at once), Pop (up from half its size, past it on a spring, and back), Scale, Fade and Scale, Scale Big, Drop (from above, bouncing to rest), Move In from Left, Right, Top, and Bottom, Skid, Spin, Pivot, Swing, and Flip; Grow and Draw for a chart's marks and a shape's outline; and Pulse, Blink, and Jiggle for emphasis, with Pop, Drop, and Swing swelling, hopping, and swaying as emphasis too. A new spring, `bouncy`, swings past its end and settles. Each is a look and a timing the engine already plays (`opacity`, `translate`, `scale`, `rotate` about an `anchor`, a `split` and a `stagger`), so nothing is new in a format, and an exit plays its entrance back.
    - **Named as a presentation app names them** in the cue's Add Motion on every editor (`scaena_core::words::PRESETS`, `Words.swift`, held together by core's words test).
    - **An older deck takes them as it opens**: a bundle's copy of a shipped theme from before gains the builds it lacks, each whose duration, easing, and color the copy names, with the springs they spring on, as it gains the layout library, every slide drawing as it did (`scaena_ops::theme::library`, `Library::builds`).
    - Tests: the engine's `the_shipped_builds_move_as_a_presentation_apps_do` (Appear takes no time, Typewriter brings a letter every 40 ms, Blink dims to 15%, Pop overshoots, Jiggle turns both ways, Drop bounces past its place, Flip and Spin turn, and each rests when its cue ends); the shipped themes offering the same builds and springs; the library bringing them into an older Daybreak, with `bouncy`, and nothing more once taken; the authoring guide's table and the motion-pass skill naming them.
- [x] 2.113 Mask with Shape: an image shown inside an oval or a shape of the library, as a presentation app masks a picture (the parent session, 2026-10-13, at Jay's "the beauty and elegance of Keynote"; Keynote's Mask with Shape, PowerPoint's Crop to Shape, from the shape library of 2.106).
    - Done. **An image's `mask`** (deck format 0.24, SPEC §3.3) is `ellipse` or a polygon of the shape library by its name (`star`, `hexagon`, `diamond`, `right-arrow`, …): the image shows only inside it, fitted to what shows as `radius` rounds it (the box under `cover` and `fill`, the drawn image under `contain`), in place of `radius`. A clip of the image's own layer, so every painter, the PDF, the SVG, and the video draw it with nothing new (`scaena_engine::images`). A change of mask between states is a new picture, which crossfades. Nothing is taken away, so `OLDEST_FORMAT_VERSION` stays.

- [x] 2.114 The pen: a freehand drawing on the slide, stroke by stroke, as a presentation app's drawing tool makes one (the parent session, 2026-10-13, at Jay's "the beauty and elegance of Keynote"; Keynote's Drawing with Apple Pencil, PowerPoint's Draw tab).
    - Done. **P takes up the pen**, in the browser, on the Mac, and on the iPad; P again or Escape puts it down. Each drag draws a stroke, its ink shown as it goes in the color and width the drawing will have: the theme's accent at its thick stroke (else its regular), round at its ends and joins. On the Mac and the iPad, Insert › Drawing and the toolbar's Shape menu take it up too, and on the iPad a finger and the Pencil draw alike.
    - **A stroke let go is smoothed** (`scaena_ops::ink::smooth`): the fewest of its points that keep within a pixel of where it went (Ramer–Douglas–Peucker, a pixel of a 1920-wide slide shown at half its size), joined by a Catmull–Rom curve through them as cubic Béziers, each piece's handles scaled by its length so that none reaches past a third of it: points kept close where the hand turned and far where it ran straight draw no hook. A press alone draws a dot.
    - **The strokes since the pen was taken up are one drawing.** The first is a new path shape on its own `view`, unfilled, named Drawing, placed by a `rect` around what it draws, entering in the state shown and kept to its slide (PLAN 3.24). Each after it is one more curve of its `path`: its `view` grows to take it in, and a `place` puts its box where the view now draws, so what it drew before stays where it was, wherever the drawing was moved since (`scaena_ops::ink::inking`, `Session::inking`, `Player.inking`, the C ABI's `inking`, `ScaenaSession.inking`). A drawing turned, given another path or view by a state, or placed by its container takes no more: the next stroke starts a drawing of its own. Each stroke is one patch, one step to undo, the drawing selected.
    - **One object.** A drawing moves, resizes, turns, and builds as one, and the Draw build (PLAN 2.112) draws its strokes on in the order they were drawn. Nothing is new in the format.
    - Tests: ops' `a_hands_stroke_is_smoothed_to_the_fewest_points_that_keep_its_way` (a tremor smoothed away, a V's corner kept, a circle kept round, points kept unevenly drawn with no handle past a third of its piece, a press a dot), `a_curves_bounds_take_in_where_it_turns_back`, and `numbers_are_written_as_short_as_they_read`; the session's `a_stroke_of_the_pen_is_a_drawing_that_later_strokes_join` (a new drawing in the accent, round at its ends, unfilled, where it was drawn; a second stroke one more curve, its box grown with what it drew first where it was; moved, a dot still where it was pressed; a stroke onto what is no drawing another drawing; no point refused); the C ABI's `a_stroke_of_the_pen_is_drawn_as_the_browser_draws_it`; ScaenaKit's `aStrokeOfThePenIsADrawingThatLaterStrokesJoin`; and the walk `pen`: P takes up the pen, a stroke's ink shows as it goes and is a drawing where it was drawn, a second stroke joins it, each undone in one step, and Escape puts the pen down.
- [x] 2.115 Shadows: an object casts a shadow under it, the theme's soft, raised, or floating, as a presentation app's objects do (the parent session, 2026-10-13, at Jay's "the beauty and elegance of Keynote"; Keynote's and PowerPoint's Shadow).
    - Done. **A node's `shadow`** (deck format 0.25, SPEC §3.3) names one of the theme's shadows, or is one written out, `{ x, y, blur, color, opacity }`, which W300 flags outside `overrides`. A theme's shadows are `tokens.shadow` (theme format 0.16): the shipped themes' `soft`, `raised`, and `floating` fall 6, 12, and 24 straight down, blurred 20, 36, and 64, at 0.18, 0.24, and 0.3 in black, and a bundle's older copy of one takes them as it opens (`scaena_ops::theme::library`). Nothing is taken away, so neither oldest version moves.
    - **What casts one, and where** (`scaena_engine::shadows`): a filled rectangle under its rounded box, an ellipse under itself (an oval's a circle's shadow stretched to it, so its outline is the oval's), an image under what shows, rounded or in an oval as it is, and a container's panel. A line, an outline, a polygon, a path, a text, a chart, and an image in another mask cast none, as a box's shadow would misdraw them. It is drawn first in the layer of what casts it, so it moves, turns, fades, and builds with it, and between two states it moves part by part, fading in or out where one side casts none.
    - **One op, every painter** (SPEC §6): `Op::Shadow`, a rounded rectangle blurred by a Gaussian, is vello_cpu's `fill_blurred_rounded_rect` and vello's `draw_blurred_rounded_rect`, the same analytic blur on the CPU and the GPU; with no blur it is a fill. The PDF and the SVG carry it as the CPU painter's pixels, its alpha kept, where neither has a blur of its own that draws the same (SPEC §10).
    - **The inspector offers it** for a shape, an image, and a container, the theme's shadows by name, Shadow in plain words, one `choose` written where it lives, in the browser, on the Mac, and on the iPad; ⌥⌘C copies it with the rest of a look.

- [x] 2.116 Each build where a presentation app offers it: an entrance and an exit offer the builds that start from a look, and emphasis those that go to one and back, as Keynote's Build In, Action, and Build Out and PowerPoint's Entrance, Emphasis, and Exit offer theirs (the parent session, 2026-10-13, after 2.112 offered Pulse as an entrance and Fade for emphasis).
    - Done. **The inspector offers a node's emphasis** beside its entrance and exit (`scaena_core::choices`): Build In, Emphasis, and Build Out, each the theme's motion presets of its kind, one `choose` written where it lives.     - **The cue's Add a motion** offers each group its own kind in the browser (`web/src/cue.ts`), and Add Motion on the Mac and the iPad offers emphasis only from those (`motionOffers`); the Animate tab's Builds shows Emphasis between Build In and Build Out.

- [x] 2.117 Adjust Image as a presentation app's: an image's exposure, contrast, saturation, and warmth, each a slider, as Keynote's Adjust Image and PowerPoint's Picture Corrections and Color set a picture's (the parent session, 2026-10-13, at Jay's "the beauty and elegance of Keynote").
    - Done. **An image's `adjust`** (deck format 0.26, SPEC §3.3): `{ exposure, contrast, saturation, warmth }`, each from -1 to 1, where 0, or none, leaves the picture as it is. Exposure, warmth, and saturation work in linear light, as a camera's would: exposure 1 is two stops brighter, warmth scales red and blue apart as a white balance does, and saturation -1 is black and white, each color at its own lightness. Contrast works about sRGB's middle, -1 a flat gray. Alpha stays as it is. Nothing is taken away, so neither oldest version moves.
    - **One function, every painter** (`scaena_core::adjust`, in `scaena-pixels`): `f32` arithmetic over tables made by `libm`, the same bytes on every target. The display list names the adjusted pixels by the image's id and the adjustment in thousandths, `sha256:…+adjust(e,c,s,w)` (SPEC §6), and the painters' store (`scaena_paint::Assets::image`) makes them from the image's own the first time a frame names them, keeping the latest eight within 64 megapixels. So vello on the GPU, vello_cpu, the PNG, the PDF, and the SVG all draw the same pixels, and the PDF carries an adjusted photo as its pixels, not its file. An image with no adjustment is named and drawn exactly as before.
    - **Between two states** an adjustment fades into the next where the picture stands, as its box moves: the new pixels over the old ones, a JPEG over itself as it was, since it hides what is under it, and a PNG, which may not, as the old ones fade out.
    - **The inspector offers Adjust Image** for an image: Exposure, Contrast, Saturation, and Warmth, each a slider from -1 to 1 in the browser and in the Mac's and the iPad's Format tab, set when let go, one `choose` written where it lives, one step to undo; let go at the middle, it takes the adjustment away. ⌥⌘C copies them with the rest of a look.
    - Tests: `scaena-pixels`' adjust tests (none leaves a picture as it is; an adjustment names its pixels and is read back; saturation at its least is black and white, each color at its lightness; exposure brightens and darkens, one stop doubling the light; contrast spreads or flattens; warmth moves white toward orange or blue; halfway is half of each); the engine's `an_adjusted_image_names_its_pixels_and_fades_from_one_adjustment_to_another`; the painters' `an_adjusted_asset_is_the_images_pixels_adjusted`; the torture deck's cases 61 and 62, `adjust` and `adjust-next@0.5`, held to golden display lists and rasters by every painter, the GPU's within §13.5 on CI's Mac; and the walk `adjust`: the four sliders offered in a person's words, Saturation let go at its least turning the photo gray where it was most colorful and leaving the slide beside it, Warmth warming the gray, each one patch and one step of undo.

    - Done. **An image's `stroke`** (SPEC §3.3) is its border: stroked on the outline of what shows as a shape's is, its `paint` and `width` the theme's (`scaena_engine::shapes::Border`). The time of day stands beside it, as the browser's locale or the system writes it.

Each ink takes its share of white away under what black leaves, `v × k / 255` rounded as zune-jpeg rounds it, in integers alone, so the pixels are the same natively and in the browser; YCCK's first three go through the YCbCr arithmetic first. No color profile is applied, as for every photo (SPEC §3.3). Nothing changes in the format.

The PDF carries the file itself, its inks inverted as Adobe's marker means them (a `Decode` array), so a viewer draws the picture its inks print through a profile of its own, near the slide and never its negative.

- [x] 2.129 By Bullet: a list built one paragraph a click, as Keynote's By Bullet and PowerPoint's By Paragraph build one, offered beside Word by Word in Build In and Build Out and in the cue's Add a motion (2026-10-14, a tester's gap beside Keynote and PowerPoint: a teacher had to split a list into separate text boxes by hand).
    - Done. **A click is a state** (SPEC §2.2), so a bullet a click is a step for each bullet. A text's `paragraphs` (deck format 0.27, SPEC §3.9) says which paragraphs a state draws, counted from 1: one, or the first and last of a run; without it, all. The text is laid out whole in every state, so no bullet moves as the others arrive (`TextLayout::keeping`), and a reader hears only what is drawn (`scaena_core::reading`). Between two steps the bullets both draw stay, and one drawn anew enters by the text's own entrance, one no longer drawn leaves by its exit, as a chart's marks enter while it stays (`scaena_engine::sample`'s `Paragraphs` track). This keeps the model's one rule, that a click is a state, rather than a click-count on a motion: the strip, Play, the remote, the PDF, and the video all see each bullet as the step it is.
    - **A split by `paragraphs`** (theme format 0.17) brings a text's paragraphs in one after another within one cue, and the shipped themes' `bullets`, By Bullet, rises each in; a bundle's older copy of a shipped theme takes it as it opens.
    - **Chosen as a text's entrance or exit**, by the inspector's `choose` or the cue's `apply_preset`, By Bullet makes the steps in the same patch (`scaena_core::builds`): in, the state the text enters in draws its first paragraph with words and a step after it each one more, the last all, so the states after show it whole as before; out, after the last state that shows it, a step for each one fewer, then one it leaves in. Each step is the slide's, named after it (`revenue-2`), in the beat of the state it follows; one undo takes the build and its steps back. A list is built in by bullet and out by bullet, each once. Offered for a text of two or more paragraphs with words, in the browser and on the Mac and the iPad, which go through the same patch.
    - **Taken back as All at Once takes it back**: another entrance or exit chosen, or none, draws the text whole again in each state of that build, and each step that said no more than its paragraphs goes, out of its beat too (`unbuilt` in `scaena_core::patch`); a step given more keeps that. And typing keeps each step's paragraphs in step with the text, as it keeps its `list` (`scaena_core::lists::redrawn`): a bullet added where a step shows only the first is drawn there as it is typed, and the steps after draw as many more.
    - Tests: core's `the_paragraphs_with_words_are_what_a_build_by_bullet_brings_in` and `a_build_by_bullet_puts_each_paragraph_on_a_click_of_its_own` (in and out, the blank paragraph kept with the one before it, nothing more once built); `a_build_by_bullet_is_taken_back_by_another_and_a_list_built_in_builds_out_too` (out after in, then each taken back, the deck as it was, a step given more kept); `typing_in_a_list_built_by_bullet_keeps_its_steps_in_step` and `an_edit_keeps_the_paragraphs_drawn_in_step`; `by_bullet_is_offered_for_a_text_of_paragraphs` (Build In and Build Out beside Word by Word, for a text of two or more paragraphs with words alone); `ends_are_a_lines_and_paragraphs_run_forward`; the engine's `a_list_builds_by_bullet_a_paragraph_a_click` (laid out whole, each step drawing its paragraphs, the new one rising by the entrance while the rest stay, one leaving by the exit, and a split by paragraphs staggering them in one cue); and the walk `builds`, By Bullet offered in Build In, Build Out, and Add an animation…, chosen on a three-paragraph note: two steps added, each drawing one more, the second's cue rising its paragraph in; Build Out's By Bullet then adding three steps after `mix`; Rise chosen taking the steps back, the note entering whole; each one undo.
- [x] 2.130 Room first, then the highlighter: the generic scanner, the C family, and JSON, in `scaena_core::code` with no dependency (Jay's request of 2026-10-14: "User can paste source code into a text box and we detect (or user selects) language and we format and color code the code - great for tech conferences."; ADR-0029, proposed; done 2026-10-15). It begins code on a slide, 2.130–2.145; where a call is Jay's, the work goes on with ADR-0029's defaults.
    - Done. **The room, measured first.** CI measured both modules at the head of PR #345 (main and the inserts batch) on 2026-10-14. The WASM engine: 8,480,387 bytes, 2,849,788 gzipped (SPEC §15: at most 3,000,000), so 150,212 bytes of room: just over the 150 KB below which ADR-0029 would hand the language tables over from the start, and about 70 KB once the top of its 40–80 KB estimate is spent. The player's engine, in a single-file export: 6,019,100 bytes, 2,209,576 gzipped. So 2.132 measures what the highlighter costs (below). Nothing calls `highlight` yet, so neither module grows here.
    - **The languages Scaena knows** (`code::LANGUAGES`), in the order of their names, which `language` finds by binary search, in any case: each by its name, its aliases, what a person calls it, its files' extensions, and the programs a script's first line runs it with. They are `c`, `cpp`, `csharp`, `dart`, `go`, `java`, `javascript`, `json`, `json5`, `jsonc`, `jsx`, `kotlin`, `objc`, `plain`, `protobuf`, `rust`, `scala`, `swift`, `tsx`, `typescript`, and `zig`. `lang` says how a name colors: by its own scanner (`Known`), not at all (`Plain`), or, for a name Scaena does not know, by the generic scanner (`Generic`).
    - **The scanners.** `highlight(text, lang)` gives a text's `Token { end, class }`s, in order, together the whole text; a `Class` is plain or one of 16 kinds: keyword, type, function, string, number, constant, comment, operator, punctuation, property, attribute, tag, variable, meta, inserted, and deleted. Three scanners, written by hand from tables:
        - **The C family's**, which a `Spec` drives for each of C, C++, C#, Go, Java, JavaScript with JSX, TypeScript, TSX, Kotlin, Swift, Rust, Dart, Scala, Zig, Objective-C, and Protocol Buffers: sorted tables of keywords, types, constants, and the variables a language defines, searched by binary search; what a declaration names (`fn`, `class`); members after a `.`; a word's case (`MAX_LEN`, `HashMap`) and what follows it (`parse(`, `useState<string>(`, Kotlin's `apply {`, and Swift's `.map {` outside a condition); C#'s attributes in brackets and the names its types declare (`string Name`); line and block comments, nested where the language nests them; strings by their openers, with escapes, interpolation (`${…}`, `$name`, `\(…)`, C#'s `{…}` and its format, `{price:C2}`), and raw strings by `#`s or a delimiter; Rust's lifetimes and macros, C++'s digit separators, the preprocessor, attributes and annotations, regular expressions where an operand may start, and JSX, with TSX's type parameters and arguments (`<T,>(…) =>`, `<List<Item> …>`).
        - **JSON's**, which reads JSON with comments and JSON5 as leniently: a key is a property, and `Infinity` and `NaN` are numbers.
        - **The generic scanner's**: strings in `"`, `'`, and backticks, numbers, comments (`//`, `/* */`, and `#`, `;`, and `--` where a word could start), brackets, and operators.
    - **What each keeps to.** It tells bytes apart by ASCII alone, so every token ends on a character's boundary and nothing reads the toolchain's Unicode tables. It is linear and total: each step reads a byte or closes what one opened, and a look ahead is bounded (a regular expression's 256 bytes, type arguments' and C#'s formats' 64). What never closes runs to the end in its class, and a string that cannot run past its line ends at the line's end. Interpolations, JSX elements, and JSX expressions stand at most 8 open inside one another (`MAX_NESTING`), and the lines past the first 1,000 are one plain token (`MAX_LINES`). Neighbors of one class are one token, and whitespace goes to the token before it.
    - **A second reading**, of real code in each language, mended what the first misread, and the samples hold each. TSX's generic arrow (`<T,>(…) =>`) read as an element, which made the rest of the file its text. After `a++`, a `/` opened a regular expression. C++'s `-> int` read as a member. C#'s attributes read as types or calls, and the names its types declare (`string Name`) and an interpolation's format as types. Swift's `.map {` read as a property. Rust's `pair.0.1` read as one number, and `collect::<Vec<&str>>()` as no call. JSON5's `5.` left its point plain. A run of Swift's `#`s, counted again at each of them, is counted once.

Handing them over saves 5,802 bytes gzipped, since the scanners are mostly code. The player's module, with every table compiled in, is 6,175,239 bytes, 2,275,693 gzipped. 2.134's six languages cost 3,378 bytes gzipped each, about 2,800 with their tables handed over, so 2.135's twelve would leave some 38 KB of room. Measured again once 2.135 had landed beneath it, by `cargo build -p scaena-wasm` alone and `wasm-bindgen`: the editor's module 8,735,235 bytes, 2,952,894 gzipped, so 47,106 bytes of room under SPEC §15; the player's 6,227,368, 2,298,047 gzipped. That room is what 2.133 and the tasks after it have: a language more is about 2 KB.

A scanner's words, its keywords, types, and constants, are sorted tables written with `words!` and listed in its module's `WORDS`. Native builds and the player's module compile them in (`scaena-core`'s `code-tables` feature, which the engine's `code-tables` asks for beside `hyphenation`). The editor's module leaves them out: each scanner's tables are a file, `crates/scaena-core/code/<file>.txt` (`clike`, `css`, `dockerfile`, `graphql`, `hcl`, `makefile`, `powershell`, `python`, `ruby`, `shell`, `sql`; a Dockerfile reads `powershell` and `shell` too, a Makefile `shell`, and JSON, YAML, TOML, a diff, INI, HTML, and XML read no table of words), which `just bless` writes from the tables compiled in. The worker hands the engine a loader (`setCodeTables`, `web/src/code-tables.ts`), which the engine asks for every file a language reads (`tables::files`) the first time a code text in it lays out, holds to its SHA-256 (`scaena_engine::code::TABLES`), and keeps. A file that cannot be had, or is not the engine's, is the frame's error that names it, never code colored by less than its tables. The smoke page and the Node glue hand the files over from the repository; a single-file export's worker is built with `web/src/no-code-tables.ts`.
    - **Format.** The theme's `code: {role, tab, syntax}` (theme format 0.18, SPEC §3.6): `role`, the text role code that names none is set in, else the theme's `code` role, else `body` (`Theme::code_role`); `tab`, the columns between stops, 1 to 16, 4 where unset; and `syntax`, each of the 16 kinds' `{color, weight, italic}`. A role or a color it lacks is E102 in the theme file. The format only adds, so the oldest theme format stays.
    - **Colored at layout** (`Theme::code_looks`). The engine reads a code text's tokens as it lays the text out and pushes each token's look over its span, its words as written. A kind's color is the theme's, else the one `palette::syntax` derives from the accent: keywords and tags the accent, numbers and constants `accent-2` where it is 45° or more from it, else the accent turned 60°, strings, types, functions, properties, and variables the accent turned 150°, 210°, 270°, 330°, and 90°, comments and meta the muted color, operators and punctuation a third of the way from the text's color to it, a diff's lines green and red, and grays on a neutral accent. Plain words take the text's own look, so `style.color` colors them. Every color is held on its ground, the surface (the card's fill becomes the ground with the card, 2.136), by the least step of Oklch lightness that reads (`palette::held`): 4.5:1, and keywords and tags 7:1 where the ground allows (`held_strong`); a color with alpha is taken as it lies there. A keyword is set at 600 at least, and a comment in its family's italic face where it has one. A change of color shapes nothing apart; a change of weight or italic is a change of face.
    - **Lint.** Contrast judges code at the colors its tokens are drawn in, and offers code no `style/color` fix: its hint says its colors are the theme's code colors, held to read on the surface. I403, new (info), finds code in a language no scanner of its own colors, one finding a text and language where the language is written, with every state it shows so in, and names the language within two letters of it that Scaena knows (`pyhton`, Did you mean `python`?).

G.22's script fonts and Insert's code key on `Theme::code_role()`: a role named `code` that code is not set in takes the script's fallback, and Insert's text in the code role is code in `plain`.
