- [ ] 2.133 Paste, tidy, and the language: a chip on the block, one click to change it (ADR-0029; after 2.132).
    - **Detection** (`code::detect`): Named, or strictly Likely, scored in integers, ties going by `LANGUAGES`' order. Its hints are fences, shebangs, file names, `text/html` classes, and VS Code's `vscode-editor-data` mode, which Rust reads from Chromium's custom data on the Mac's pasteboard.
    - **Tidying** (`code::tidy`): line ends, a byte-order mark, U+00A0, fences, tabs, trailing spaces, blank lines at either end, and the indentation the lines share, with the rule for a first line cut at its first character. JSON is reindented only where it is minified. It reports curly quotes and missing glyphs by line. `cells::read` refuses text whose every row's first cell is empty, and text that `detect` names.
    - **Paste.** `clipboard::of_text` detects first and hands code to `of_code`, sized to its lines at the role's size. A paste into a code text being typed in is tidied and indented to its line (`codeKey`'s paste). A paste over the whole text of a Plain Text code text takes its language through `Session::typed`, which decides `typing` before it appends the `choose`.
    - **Insert's code** is a Plain Text code block, typed in as it lands: no dialog and no sheet.
    - **The language**: a chip on the block, and the inspector's Language (`Source::Languages`).
    - **Commands**: Copy Code; Paste as Text; a source file dropped pastes as words, its name the hint. In the browser, on the Mac, and on the iPad.
    - Tests: detection (the corpus: code Named or Likely, prose nothing; a single line code only where it says so; a fence, a file name, or VS Code's mode names the language; Chromium's custom data gives VS Code's mode; ties by the table's order); tidy (idempotent, tabs to stops, U+00A0, the four cut-first-line cases, JSON reindented only where minified with every token as written); `cells.rs`, tab-indented code no sheet; `keys.rs`, a paste takes its line's indentation; the session (pasted code a code text sized to its lines; a Plain Text block takes its paste's language and still grows, in one `type` run; Copy Code's words); `ffi.rs`, the paste the browser's patch; the walks `code.mjs` (paste) and `paste.mjs` as before; ScaenaKit's `ClipboardTests` and `CodeTests`; the iPad's `OpeningTests` screenshot; `window.sh`'s code-pasted shot.
- [x] 2.134 More languages: Python, Ruby, Shell, Console, PowerShell, SQL (ADR-0029; after 2.130; done 2026-10-15).
    - Done. **Scanners of their own**, written by hand from tables as 2.130's are, a file each in `scaena_core::code`. Each name moves from the generic scanner to its own: a reviewed golden diff for any deck that names it.
        - **Python's** (`python.rs`): strings by their prefixes, `r`, `b`, `u`, `f`, `t`, or two of them, in any case, in one quote or three. An f-string's or a t-string's replacement field is code, in which strings take the field's own quotes and fields nest (PEP 701): its conversion (`!r`) an operator, and its format spec a string that holds fields of its own (`{value:>{width}}`); `{{`, `}}`, and a named escape's braces (`\N{EM DASH}`) are the string's. Decorators are attributes, and the module a statement's `from` names is plain, as any module's name is (`from __future__`). The soft keywords are keywords only where they act as keywords: `match` and `case` first in a statement whose line ends with a `:`, `_` in a case's pattern, and `type` before the name of the type it declares.
        - **Ruby's** (`ruby.rs`): strings that run over lines, `#{…}`, `#@name`, and `#$name` interpolated where they are; heredocs, `<<~ID`, `<<-ID`, and `<<ID`, the identifier maybe in quotes, each body read from the line after its opener to its terminator, two on a line in turn; `%`-literals by any delimiter and its pair, nested (`%w[…]`, `%i(…)`, `%q{…}`, `%r|…|`); symbols and a hash's keys as constants; regular expressions where an operand may start or as a command's argument; a method's `?` and `!`; what `def` names, its receiver and an operator too (`def <=>(other)`); a method called with no parentheses (`puts "hi"`, `has_many :posts`, `puts <<~EOS`, `warn $0`) or given a block (`expect {`, and `before do` first on its line) as a function; and `=begin` … `=end` and `__END__`.
        - **Shell's** (`shell.rs`), for bash, sh, and zsh: `#` a comment only where a word starts, so `a#b`, `${#x}`, and `$#` are none; `'…'`, `$'…'`, and `"…"`, which expands variables, `${…}`, `$(…)`, `$((…))`, and backquotes, each over lines; arithmetic, whose names are variables (`$((count + 1))`, `16#ff`); heredocs, `<<EOF`, `<<-EOF` past its tabs, and `<<'EOF'`, which expands nothing; and command position: a command's first word a function, a keyword (`if`, `export`, `return`), or a constant (`true`), an assignment before it a variable, after `;`, `|`, `&&`, `(`, `then`, `do`, and a command that runs another (`sudo`, `env`, `xargs`). `for NAME in`, `case WORD in` and its patterns to their `)`, the names `export` and `local` declare, a test's `-f` and `-eq` in `[ … ]` and `[[ … ]]`, and redirections (`2>&1` whole, the file after one plain) read as what they are.
        - **Console's** (`console.rs`): a prompt at a line's start is meta: `$`, `#`, `%`, or `>`, maybe after who and where (`user@host:~/code$`, `[user@host code]#`, `~/code $`, `bash-5.2$`) and a virtual environment's name, or PowerShell's `PS C:\>`. The command after it reads as Shell, or as PowerShell after a PowerShell prompt, over the lines what it opens holds open (a quote, a heredoc, a `\` or a `|` at a line's end, an `if` before its `fi`), each such line's continuation prompt (`> `, zsh's `for> `, `>> `) meta: Shell's and PowerShell's scanners stop at each line's end and go on where the next line's prompt ends. Every other line is output, and plain.
        - **PowerShell's** (`powershell.rs`): keywords and operators in any case; `"…"`, expanding `$name` and `$(…)` past backtick escapes, `'…'`, and here-strings, `@"…"@` and `@'…'@`; `#` where a token starts, and `<# … #>`; `$name`, `$env:PATH`, `${any name}`, and `@name` splatted as variables, and `$true`, `$false`, and `$null` as constants; a word where a command's name stands (`Get-ChildItem`, `git`, `./build.ps1`) as a function, as a cmdlet's `Verb-Noun` is anywhere, and the command's arguments plain, a path going on after a variable (`$PSScriptRoot\lib.ps1`, `${PWD}:/data`); `-eq` and `-like` as operators, and `-Path` and a program's `--amend` as parameters, attributes; `[string]` and `[int[]]` as types, after another's `]` too (`[Parameter()][string]`), `[Parameter(Mandatory)]` as an attribute, its named arguments and a hashtable's keys as properties, and an enum's members as constants.
        - **SQL's** (`sql.rs`): keywords, types, and constants in any case; strings with their quotes doubled, and after a prefix (`N'…'`, `E'…'`, in which a backslash escapes); names in `"…"`, `` `…` ``, and `[…]`; dollar-quoted bodies, `$$…$$` and `$tag$…$tag$`, one string each; `--`, `/* */`, and `#` first on a line. A name called is a function (`COUNT(*)`, and `LEFT(` where a keyword shares a function's name), a type with its size a type (`VARCHAR(255)`), as is a cast's (`::int`), and a table's name before its columns a name (`CREATE TABLE orders (`, `INSERT INTO t(a)`, `AS g(n)`); `@name`, `$1`, `:name`, and a `?` that stands for a value are variables.
    - **The languages** (`code::LANGUAGES`): `console` (`sh-session`, `shell-session`, `shellsession`), `powershell` (`posh`, `ps`, `ps1`, `pwsh`), `python` (`py`, `python3`), `ruby` (`rb`), `shell` (`bash`, `sh`, `shell-script`, `shellscript`, `zsh`), and `sql` (`mysql`, `pgsql`, `plpgsql`, `postgres`, `postgresql`, `sqlite`, `tsql`), each with its files' extensions and the programs a script's first line runs it with: `python`, `python2`, `python3`, and PyPy; `ruby` and `jruby`; `bash`, `sh`, `zsh`, `dash`, `ash`, and `ksh`; `pwsh` and `powershell`; and `psql` and `sqlite3`, which run a script only through `env -S` with their flags.
    - **What each keeps to**, as 2.130's scanners do: ASCII classes; linear and total, a look ahead bounded (a soft keyword's line and a regular expression 256 bytes; a prompt, a name in brackets, and a named escape 128; a type in brackets and a dollar quote's tag 64; a heredoc's quoted delimiter and PowerShell's `${…}` no further than the close they look for on their line); at most 8 replacement fields, interpolations, and substitutions open inside one another, and 8 heredocs waiting for their bodies; what never closes runs to the end in its class.
    - **A second reading**, of other real code in each language (a FastAPI handler, a Rails controller, a CI script, a PowerShell class, an analytics query, and console sessions), mended what the first misread, and the samples hold each. PowerShell's `.` as a path (`-Path .`) read as punctuation, and an attribute's named argument (`[CmdletBinding(SupportsShouldProcess)]`) as a function. SQL's table named before its columns (`CREATE TABLE … orders (`, `REFERENCES customers (id)`, `AS g(n)`) read as a function, a placeholder `?` as an operator, and a column named `action` as a keyword. Ruby's `puts <<~A, <<~B` left `puts` plain.
    - **A review** found two scanners reading a line again from each opener on it, in time that grew with the square of the line's length: Shell's quoted heredoc delimiter read the rest of its line before it counted the heredocs waiting, and PowerShell's `${…}` read to its line's end before it looked for its `}`. Each now looks no further than its close, as Ruby's quoted heredoc identifier does too, and a quarter of a megabyte of either on one line reads at once where it took up to half a minute. The review's own reading of real code mended what that misread, and the samples hold each: Python's `from __future__` read as a variable; Ruby's `warn $0`, `expect {`, and `before do` left the method plain; PowerShell's `.5` read as a command, `$PSScriptRoot\lib.ps1` and `${PWD}:/data` split at an operator, a type after an attribute's `]` (`[Parameter()] [string]`) as plain, `--amend` as an operator and a parameter, `--name=web`'s value as a command, and an enum's members as functions; a console's `~/code $` prompt as output; and SQL's `STABLE` as a name.

        - **TOML's** (`toml.rs`): `[tables]` and `[[arrays of tables]]` first on their lines, their names types, dotted and quoted; keys, bare, quoted, and dotted, properties, an inline table's too; basic strings with escapes and literal strings, each ending at its line's end, and both in three quotes over lines, a quote or two before the close their own; numbers with underscores, in hexadecimal, octal, and binary, floats with exponents, `inf` and `nan` signed or not, and dates and times, offset or local, a date's time after the one space between them; `true` and `false` constants; and arrays over lines.
        - **A Dockerfile's** (`dockerfile.rs`): instructions in any case keywords, with `FROM`'s `AS` and a health check's `CMD` and `NONE`; `RUN`'s, `CMD`'s, `ENTRYPOINT`'s, and a health check's command read as Shell over the lines the escape joins (`shell::instruction`), the comments and blank lines among them dropped as Docker drops them, so the command goes on after each as it was; or as PowerShell, where `SHELL` names it or the escape is a backtick; a heredoc that is the whole command (`RUN <<EOF`) its script, and one in another instruction (`COPY <<EOF /etc/x`) a string to its terminator; the exec form's JSON strings strings; flags (`--mount=…`) attributes; `ARG`'s and `ENV`'s names variables and `LABEL`'s keys properties, in quotes too, before their `=`; `$name` and `${name:-x}` variables, in double quotes too; quotes over the lines the escape joins; digits numbers, but an image's tag a name (`FROM node:20`, `COPY --from=alpine:3.20`); and parser directives at the file's start meta, the escape one read.
        - **A diff's** (`diff.rs`): `diff --git`, `index`, `---`, `+++`, modes, renames, `Index:`, and `Binary files` meta; a hunk's header meta and the function after it plain, its counts saying how many lines the hunk holds, so its lines that look like a file's header are taken away and added; and a line what its first character makes it, `+` inserted, `-` deleted, a space context, plain, and `\ No newline at end of file` a comment, outside a hunk too but for the headers, a mail's message from its headers (`From `, `Subject: `) to its patch, and its `-- `, each line's token holding its break.
        - **HCL's** (`hcl.rs`), as Terraform writes it: a block's word a keyword and its labels strings; an attribute's name a property; `true`, `false`, and `null` constants, a type's name a type (`list(string)`), a function called a function, a reference's root (`var`, `local`, `module`, `data`, `each`, `count`) a variable and what a `.` reaches a property; `for`, `in`, `if`, and a directive's words keywords; strings as templates, `${…}` and `%{…}` holding an expression and `$${` and `%%{` the string's own; heredocs, `<<EOT` and `<<-EOT`, to the line that is their delimiter alone; and `#`, `//`, and `/* */` comments.
        - **GraphQL's** (`graphql.rs`): a definition's words keywords, with `on`, `implements`, and `repeatable`; an operation's or a fragment's name a function and a type's a type; fields and aliases properties, arguments' and parameters' names attributes, `$variables` variables, directives attributes, and a fragment's spread a function; a name where a type stands (after a `:`, `on`, `implements`, `&`, and a union's `=` and `|`, and in a list type) a type; strings and block strings; `true`, `false`, `null`, an enum's values, and a directive's locations constants; and an input object's keys properties.
        - **A Makefile's** (`makefile.rs`): a rule's targets functions, a special target (`.PHONY`) a keyword, its prerequisites plain, and an order-only `|` an operator; its recipe, a line after its tab or what follows its `;`, read as Shell after its `@`, `-`, and `+` (`shell::recipe`), Make expanding first, so `$(CC)` and `$@` are variables in quotes too and `$$` is the shell's `$`; an assignment's name (`=`, `:=`, `::=`, `?=`, `+=`, `!=`) a variable, a target's too, before any `;`, and the `export`, `override`, `private`, or `unexport` before it a keyword (`test: private CFLAGS += -g`); `define` to its `endef`; `$(NAME)`, `${NAME}`, a substitution, and `$@` variables, and a function called (`$(patsubst …)`) its name a function and its brackets and commas punctuation; directives keywords; `#` comments, but for an escaped `\#`; and a `\` that ends a line going on.
        - **INI's** (`ini.rs`), for `.properties`, `.cfg`, `.conf`, and `.editorconfig` too: `[sections]` types, a quoted part a string; `;`, `#`, and a `.properties` file's `!` comments first on a line; a key before its `=` or `:` a property; and a value a string, a number, or a constant (`true`, `yes`, `on`, and their opposites), its `${name}` and `%(name)s` variables, going on past a `\` and, as configparser reads one, onto the lines indented past its key, an `=` or a `:` in them its own (`install_requires =` and the requirements under it), where keys indented alike, as `.gitconfig` writes them, are keys.
        - **HTML's and XML's** (`markup.rs`): tags; attributes and their values, in quotes over lines or bare; entities (`&amp;`, `&#169;`, `&#x1F600;`) in text and in values; comments over lines; CDATA, its brackets meta and what it holds a string; and processing instructions and doctypes meta, an internal subset whole. In HTML, what `<script>`, `<style>`, `<textarea>`, and `<title>` hold is text to its closing tag, the last two's entities theirs.
        - **CSS's and SCSS's** (`css.rs`): each statement looked through once for the `{`, `;`, or `}` that ends it, so a rule's selector is told from a declaration; selectors' tags, classes, ids, pseudo-classes, attribute selectors' names, values, and flags (` i]`), and combinators; properties and custom properties (`--gap`); values' numbers with their units, colors (`#fff`, `#3366ccaa`) wherever they stand, functions, `url(…)` holding a string, keywords, and `!important`; and at-rules, their preludes' `and`, `not`, `only`, and `or` and features. For SCSS, `$variables` and a module's (`math.$pi`), `@mixin`, `@include`, and `@function` naming a function, `@extend` and `@at-root` naming selectors, `@extend`'s `!optional`, `!default`, `&`, `%placeholders`, a property whose block holds its parts (`font: { … }`), a map's keys, `//` comments, and `#{…}` in selectors, properties, values, and strings, a name going on after it as it began (`.#{$block}__item`). The generic scanner read a color after a space as a comment.

    - **What each keeps to**, as 2.134's scanners do: ASCII classes; linear and total, a look ahead bounded (an entity's 40 bytes, a block scalar's indicators 2); at most 8 templates, interpolations, expansions, and calls open inside one another, and 8 heredocs waiting for their bodies, after one Dockerfile instruction or one line of HCL; what never closes runs to the end in its class.

The tests hold two more: a TOML string's `\` at its line's end ran on to the next line, and a mail's `-- ` read as a line taken away.
    - **A review of time** found two scanners reading on from each word to their line's end: a Dockerfile's, at each word, each exec form's string, and each escape, and a Makefile's, at each directive (`override override …`), so a megabyte of either took minutes. Each now looks no further than what it reads, and a megabyte of each construct's openers in each language, on one line and on lines of a kilobyte, reads in about a third of a second at worst, unoptimized: SCSS's `#{` in a rule.
    - **A review**, a second reader's, read each language again in code a speaker shows (a `docker-compose.yml` with anchors and block scalars, a release workflow, a Kubernetes manifest, and a CloudFormation template; a `Cargo.toml` and a `pyproject.toml`; multi-stage, Python, and Windows Dockerfiles; a `git format-patch` mail with a rename and a binary file; a Terraform module and a Nomad job; a schema and its queries; two Makefiles; an `.editorconfig`, a `.gitconfig`, a `setup.cfg`, and Java properties; a sign-up page, an SVG, and XSLT; a site's styles; and SCSS partials), and mended what the first misread. Make read a recipe after a rule's `;` as the rule's own variable where it held an `=` (`print-%: ; @echo $*=$($*)`) and its `@` as part of its command, a rule's `private` before the variable it sets as the variable's name, and `\#` as a comment. YAML read the `:` after a JSON key (`{"a":1}`) and the value after it as one string, and the first scalar of a flow collection after a `?` as a key. SCSS read a module's variable (`math.$pi`) as a constant and `@extend`'s `!optional` as a tag. A Dockerfile read an image's tag (`FROM node:20`) as a number, and a diff the `- ` and `+ ` of a mail's message as lines taken away and added. The samples and the tests hold each. It timed a quarter of a megabyte, a megabyte, and four megabytes of each construct's openers in each language, on one line and on a thousand CRLF lines: each grows in proportion, at worst SCSS's `#{` at a fifth of a second a megabyte, unoptimized and alone. - [ ] 2.136 The card and the line numbers (ADR-0029; after 2.133; deck format 0.29, theme format 0.19).

    - **The gutter's numbers** live in `CodeLayout.gutter`, in saturating arithmetic. The card, the band, the gutter, and the code draw in that order, and the colors hold to the card's fill.

    - **The morph.** The card lerps and the gutter cross-fades; split builds keep both under the units.
    - **The inspector** offers Line Numbers, First Line Number, and Card, and ⌥⌘C copies them. `library()` brings the card and the numbers only into copies whose deck shows no code.
    - Tests: the engine's (line numbers stand beside each line, and no caret, unit, word, or reader takes them; a start near its maximum does not overflow; the card hugs the code and `align` places it; a theme with no card gets one 1.3:1 off its surface; code's words judged against the card; the contrast fix turns the card on; a code text collides by its card); core's (choices offer the fields where the node is code; looks copy them; the words' parity); `library.rs` (card and numbers brought only where no code shows); torture case 64 with its card and numbers, blessed again and reviewed.
