- [x] 4.3 The canvas by touch: a tap selects what the engine says draws there; a drag moves a node, and a handle resizes, turns, or reshapes it, each taken by a finger within 22 pt; two fingers pan and a pinch zooms; a long press offers what is done to a node (the Node menu, the clipboard, its lock); a double tap types in a text; slides move in the light table by a drag. Each is the patch the Mac's gesture makes (PLAN 3.7, 3.11, 3.14, 3.16).
    - **A finger is the pointer.** The canvas's press is the Mac's: a tap is a click, and a double tap a double click, counted by the canvas itself (`Presses`): a press that begins within UIKit's double-tap time of the one before, and a finger's reach of it. A finger goes further than a pointer before its press is a drag, and puts the caret in a text from further outside it.
    - **Handles a finger takes.** A handle is taken within 22 points, half the 44 Apple gives a finger's target, and no more than a quarter of the box's shorter side, so a finger inside a small box still moves it. The rotate handle stands further out, clear of the box's top.
    - **Two fingers.** A pinch grows the slide on the desk about the fingers (`CanvasPinch`) and two fingers moving pan it (`CanvasPan`), as the Mac's trackpad and wheel do. A second finger touching down stops the first finger's press at once, drawing back any drag it began: what two fingers do edits nothing.
    - **A long press** offers iPadOS's edit menu over what it pressed (`CanvasMenu`): Cut, Copy, and Paste, then what the Node menu does (Duplicate, Delete, Arrange, Copy and Paste Style, Lock, Insert), each the Mac's patch. What draws there on top is selected first, locked or not, so Unlock is offered; what is selected stays selected where it draws there, under another or not.
    - **Read as it is shown.** The canvas is read as a container the view's own size, holding the nodes as VoiceOver reads them, each framed by the part of it the view shows. A node zoomed out of view is not read, and nothing drawn past the view widens the frame a pinch's fingers are placed by.
    - Tests: the iPad's `TouchTests` on B1's cover, a gesture at a time: a tap selects the title, a pinch zooms and another takes the whole slide back, offering no menu; a double tap types in the title and a tap where nothing draws stops; a drag moves it; a long press offers its menu, whose Duplicate copies it; and in the light table a slide dragged onto another moves after it.
- [x] 4.4 Typing through `UITextInput`: the software keyboard and a hardware one, an input method's composition, dictation, and Scribble, the caret and the selection read from the engine's carets; the characters' look, ⌘K's link, and a list's keys as on the Mac (PLAN 3.9, 3.10).
    - **`CanvasKeys` on the iPad** is a `UIView` that adopts `UITextInput` over the Mac's `Typing`, as the Mac's adopts `NSTextInputClient`. It lays nothing out and draws nothing: the text, the selection, the composition, the positions, the caret's and the selection's rects, the character at a point, and the lines the tokenizer counts all come from the engine's carets. It takes the keyboard only while a text is typed in, so the software keyboard comes and goes with typing, and the input system is told of each change it did not make.
    - **Keys.** While a text is typed in, a hardware keyboard's keys are the Mac's key bindings' (`wantsPriorityOverSystemBehavior`): the arrows by character, word, and line, each extending with ⇧; ⌥⌫ and ⌘⌫; Escape; Tab and ⇧Tab in a list; ⌘B, ⌘I, ⌘K, ⌘⇧8, ⌘⇧7, and ⌘F. The shortcuts bar's B and I are ⌘B and ⌘I.
    - **Composition, dictation, and Scribble.** An input method composes in place, dictation types what it heard, and `UIIndirectScribbleInteraction` makes the state shown's texts, those not locked, what the Pencil writes in.
    - **`Typing` gains** the boxes a range covers (`covering`), the character at a point, and the texts the Pencil may write in (`writable`), platform-neutral and tested on both.
    - Tests: the Mac's typing tests' iPad twins drive the view as the Mac's drive `NSTextInputClient` (keys, a dead key's composition, autocorrection's replacement, a hardware keyboard's moves, a list's keys, Scribble's elements); the touch UI test types in the title with XCUITest's keyboard after its double tap. Scribble itself, the software keyboard, and dictation wait on an iPad.
- [x] 4.5 The Pencil and the pointer: the Pencil's hover shows what a press would select and the handle under it; a trackpad or a mouse hovers, Shift-clicks, draws a marquee, and right-clicks as on the Mac (PLAN 3.13).

It is read from the boxes at rest (`NodeBox.holds`, `[NodeBox].under`), with nothing asked of the engine as the pointer moves, and a locked node is passed over as a click passes over it. The Mac's canvas hovers the same.

- [x] 4.6 The keyboard and VoiceOver: the Mac's commands in iPadOS's menu bar and by their keys; the canvas by keys alone, and each node an accessibility element that reads as the reader hears it (PLAN 3.17).
    - **The canvas's keys.** While no text is typed in, a press on the canvas gives the keyboard to `CanvasPresses`, a plain view with no text input, so no software keyboard comes with it; typing stopped gives the keyboard back to it. A hardware keyboard's Tab, Return, Escape, the arrows, Space, the brackets, +, and Delete go to the canvas first, mapped from UIKit's codes onto the Mac's own steps (`pressed`): Tab selects in reading order, Return goes into a container or onto a node's handles or types in a text, the arrows step a node as its drag would (a canvas unit a press on the theme's grid, as a drag goes there since PLAN 3.19, on the Mac too), and the brackets turn it. ⌘. is Escape, the system's cancel key, which a keyboard with no Escape key gives for it, the iPad's own among them; while a text is typed in, either stops typing. What the canvas does not take goes on, so Tab past either end leaves the slide.
    - **The commands.** The Mac's menus are the app's own commands, which iPadOS's menu bar lists. While no text is typed in, the Edit menu's Copy, Cut, Paste, and Select All act on the canvas.
      - A press comes to the canvas before the menu bar's keys are matched, and in the UI tests the menu's own keys did nothing there: ⌘D reached the canvas and went on, and no copy was made. So the canvas takes the Node menu's keys itself, as the browser's canvas takes ⌘D: ⌘D, ⌘G and ⇧⌘G, ⇧⌘L, ⌘] and ⌘[ with or without ⌥, ⌥⌘C and ⌥⌘V, and ⌘F and ⇧⌘F, each what its menu item does (`menuKey`).
    - **VoiceOver.** Each node is the accessibility element the Mac's canvas makes (PLAN 3.17): it reads as the reader hears it (§3.12), in reading order, and says whether it is selected.

Tab and Shift+Tab select the title and the subtitle in reading order; the arrows nudge the title a canvas unit a press, each press a step ⌘Z takes back; Return types in it and Escape stops, the keys the canvas's again; ⌘D duplicates it and ⌘Z takes the copy back; and Escape selects what holds it. Each key the canvas does not hear is said, and the test fails at its end saying every one.
      - XCUITest's keyboard on the iPad's simulator sends no key for `.return` and Ctrl+C for `.enter`; a newline is the Return key, which the test sends. Escape reached nothing in the app in any run, as a press or a key command, so where it is not heard the test presses ⌘. in its place and says so beside the results. Escape itself, the menu bar's look, and VoiceOver's speech wait on an iPad.
- [x] 4.7 Files in and out: a picture, a data file, a sheet's cells, or a clip dropped on the canvas from another app or window lands as it pastes (PLAN 3.12, 2.96); the PDF and a state's PNG go out through the share sheet and Quick Look (PLAN 3.8).
    - **In.** A drop on the canvas lands as a paste lands, on the iPad and the Mac alike. A picture or a data file lands as before (PLAN 3.22). Words land too (`Pasteboard.droppedWords`): a sheet's cells become a data source and the table they were, a clip's text the nodes it holds, and other words a text, each where it is let go.

- [x] 4.8 Presenting: Play fills an external display or an AirPlay screen and shows the presenter's view on the iPad, or fills the iPad alone, a tap or a swipe going on (PLAN 3.5).
    - **Another display.** The app declares the scene iPadOS makes for an external display or an AirPlay screen (`UIWindowSceneSessionRoleExternalDisplayNonInteractive`, `DisplaySceneDelegate`). While a deck plays, the stage is a window on that scene and the iPad shows the presenter's view: the slide as the audience sees it, the next, the notes, where the show is, and the time. A display that comes while the deck plays takes the stage, and one that goes gives it back to the iPad (`Presenting.place`). While no deck plays, the app puts no window there.
    - **The iPad alone.** With no other display, the stage fills the iPad. A tap or a swipe to the left goes on, a swipe to the right goes back, and a pinch closed ends the show; a keyboard's keys pace it as on the Mac. The presenter's view takes the same swipes and the pinch, a tap on the slide goes on, and End Show ends it. Held upright, the presenter's view puts the next and the notes under the slide.
    - **VoiceOver.** The stage is one element that reads as the slide reads (§3.12) and says where the show is ("3 of 40"). VoiceOver's swipes up and down go on and back, and its escape ends the show.
    - Tests: the iPad's `PresentingTests` play B1 on the simulator: the stage on the cover and reading as it, a swipe on and one back, a tap on, and a pinch closed ending the show. The stage on another display waits on an iPad with one; XCUITest attaches none.
- [x] 4.9 The assistant and the on-device tasks, as on the Mac, the key in the iPad's Keychain (PLAN 3.6).
    - **As on the Mac.** The assistant's panel, its conversation (`scaena-chat`, ADR-0022), and the on-device tasks are the Mac's own code, compiled for the iPad since PLAN 4.2. The on-device tasks are offered where the iPad has Apple's Foundation Models.
    - **The keys.** The iPad keeps them in its Keychain, in a sheet, since it has no Settings window. The sheet names each provider beside its field, and each of a row's buttons takes its own tap. A row of a form on the iPad otherwise takes a tap as every button's in it, so keeping a second key took the first away.
    - **No key yet.** With no key kept for the provider chosen, the panel says so and offers Add a Key…, which opens the sheet on the iPad and Settings on the Mac. The models are listed again once the sheet closes.
    - Tests: the iPad's `AssistantTests` open the assistant on B1, add a key through Add a Key…, keep another in its place, and take it away. The panel then offers a key to add again. A conversation with a provider and the on-device tasks wait on an iPad with a key and with Apple Intelligence.
- [x] 4.10 The remote (SPEC §9.5): an iPhone or an iPad drives a deck a Mac or an iPad presents, over the local network, and shows its notes and the next state. A place in the deck is a state and a time into its cue.
    - **The protocol** (ADR-0025, accepted on Jay's call, 2026-10-11). `ScaenaRemote` is a library of the ScaenaKit package with no engine in it.
      - A presenter's `RemoteServer` offers the deck by Bonjour and listens under TLS. Its pre-shared key is derived from a code of four digits.
      - A remote's `RemoteBrowser` finds presenters, and its `RemoteClient` joins one with the code.
      - Each message is JSON after its length. The presenter sends a place (`RemotePlace`): a state, its time into its cue or at rest, its notes, and it and the next drawn at rest, as PNGs. The remote sends a command (`RemoteCommand`): play, on, back, first, last, or end.
    - **Presenting.** Remote… in the Play menu turns the host on for the Mac or the iPad (`RemoteHost`), its sheet showing the code in the editor, where the audience does not see it.
      - The host follows whichever show plays (`Presenting.current`). It sends a place when the state changes or its cue comes to rest, each picture drawn once for each revision of the deck.
      - It does what a remote asks. With no show playing, play plays the deck from the slide the editor shows.
    - **The remote.** `RemoteView` lists the presenters nearby, takes the code, and then shows the slide, the next, the notes, where the show is, and the time. A tap or a swipe on the slide goes on, Back and Next are buttons, and End Show ends the show. The screen stays on while it is joined.
      - On an iPhone it is the Remote app (`apps/ipad/Remote`, the `RemoteApp` target), which holds no deck and no engine.
      - On an iPad it is the app's window Control a Presentation…, in the Play menu.
      - Each app's Info.plist names the Bonjour service and says why it uses the local network.
    - Tests: ScaenaKit's `aRemoteJoinsWithTheCodeAndDrivesTheShow`, on the Mac and the iPad's simulator, joins a server on the loopback with its code. The remote hears where the show is as it joins and as it goes, and the presenter hears what it asks. A remote with another code is never joined. CI builds the Remote app with the iPad's. A show driven from an iPhone across a room waits on the devices.
- [ ] 4.11 Undo by touch: the toolbar's Undo takes back the last step, and a long press on it offers Redo, as a presentation app's does on the iPad. *(Added on 2026-10-10: gate 4's fourth criterion asks that each edit by touch be one step of undo, and an iPad without a keyboard had no Undo to touch: no ⌘Z, and the menu bar a swipe from the top away. Built; waits on a run on an iPad.)*
    - **The button.** Undo stands beside View and Zoom (`UndoButton`). A tap undoes; a long press offers Redo, named for the step it makes again.
    - **It reads as its step.** VoiceOver and the pointer's tooltip say what a tap takes back as the Edit menu names it (`Undo Body Size`, PLAN 3.28), read again whenever the window's undo makes, takes back, or makes again a step (`UndoSteps`). With nothing to undo or redo, it is dimmed.
    - **The Mac** keeps its Edit menu and ⌘Z, as a Mac's presentation app does: its toolbar has no Undo.
    - Tests: the iPad's `TouchTests` on B1. A drag's move and a long press's Duplicate are each taken back by one tap of Undo, and a long press on Undo offers Redo, which makes the copy again.
- [ ] 4.12 Zoomed in, the keys pan and what they select comes into view, and PageDown and PageUp turn the slides from the canvas: the browser's PLAN 2.125 and 2.126 on the Mac and the iPad, which share ScaenaKit and the Mac app's sources. *(Added on 2026-10-14 from the gap hunt's batch GB, whose browser half is PLAN 2.122–2.126: zoomed in, no key moved the view, Tab selected an object out of view and left it there, and no key on the canvas changed the slide. Built; waits on a run on a Mac and an iPad.)*
    - **The arrows pan.** Zoomed in with nothing selected, an arrow moves what the desk shows a tenth of itself, and with Shift half, the slide kept on the desk, so that at its edge it goes no farther; it selects and changes nothing (`Zoom.pan(_:_:far:)`). Fitted, or with Option, the arrows go on as before.
    - **What is selected comes into view.** What Tab, Return, a selection by keys, the objects on the slide in the inspector, find, a finding, or the cue selects is brought into view: the least pan that shows it whole where it is drawn, or, larger than what shows, the least that shows nothing but it (`Zoom.reveal`, `NodeBox.extent`). A click or a tap, on what shows already, moves nothing.
    - **Slides by keys.** On the canvas, PageDown and PageUp show the next slide or step and the one before (`DeckEditor.state(from:by:)`), and the desk under the slide says which ("Slide 2 shown"), or that this is the last or the first slide, and the slides down the side scroll to keep the one shown in view, as the browser's strip does (`StateList`). On a Mac keyboard without them, fn with ↓ and ↑ gives them. On the iPad they are key commands of the canvas's own, ahead of the system's, as Escape and Return are (`CanvasPresses`), so each is heard once however the system hands it on. A text typed in keeps them, as the source pane and the fields do: the canvas takes them, as the browser's slide does, and no menu item binds them. Neither app has a keys sheet to list them in, nor a menu item for the next slide to show them beside: `apps/mac/README.md` and SPEC §9.3 say them.
    - Tests: ScaenaKit's `theArrowsPanATenthOfWhatShowsOrHalfWithShift`, `whatIsSelectedComesIntoViewTheLeastWay` (B1's subtitle and title at four times fitted, a turned box, a box past the canvas's edge), and `pageDownAndPageUpGoAlongTheDecksStates`, on the Mac and the iPad's simulator, and on the simulator `pageDownAndPageUpAreTheIPadCanvassOwnKeyCommands`, each key alone and with Shift going to the canvas ahead of the system. The iPad's `KeysTests` on B1 (`testTheKeysPanWhatIsZoomedInAndTurnTheSlides`): the toolbar's Zoom In, then with nothing selected → moves the subtitle's end a tenth of the canvas's width to the left, ← back, and Shift+→ farther, selecting nothing; PageDown shows Slide 2 and the desk says so, PageUp Slide 1, and PageUp again says this is the first slide, on an iPad: the simulator's keyboard sends no PageDown (the app's key log hears the arrows before it and no PageDown at all), so there the step keeps a picture and goes on, and the commands are held by ScaenaKit's test. The Mac's walk presses → and PageDown zoomed in and keeps what it saw.
- [x] 4.13 The window answers while the deck is linted: on the Mac and the iPad, every state is linted away from the main thread, at open and once edits stop, its findings arriving when ready. *(Added on 2026-10-16: B1's opening lint held the main thread, about a second in release and some 30 s in the iPad simulator's debug build, where the iPad's `AssistantTests` and `KeysTests` failed now and then on a window that did not answer.)*
    - **Off the main thread.** Opening a deck compiles it and lints nothing (`DeckEditor.init`); `DeckEditor.lintEvery` takes the deck as it stands from the session (`Session::laying`, `scaena_laying_new`), lays it out on a queue of its own with an engine of its own, one lint at a time (`Laying::lay`), and hands it back on the caller's actor, where the session judges it with the rules that lay nothing out (`Session::lint_laid`). The session is used from one thread only, as the C ABI asks: the laying shares the bundle's files, read only, and nothing else. The state shown is still linted at once at an edit.
    - **Never stale.** A lint of a deck changed since it began is dropped twice over: the editor counts each source compiled, and the session finds another source, another file, or another theme. One queued behind it for a deck changed since is not laid out.
    - Tests: `a_lint_laid_out_away_from_the_session_is_the_sessions_and_a_stale_one_is_dropped` holds what it finds to `lint` on every state, a thread laying it out while the session draws. ScaenaKit's `b1OpensBeforeItsEveryStateIsLintedAndTheFindingsComeAfter` and `aLintOfADeckChangedSinceItBeganIsDropped`, on the Mac and the iPad's simulator; gate 3's and 4's first-frame test also opens B1 for editing and expects no lint on the way.

### Exit criteria (gate 4)
1. Same bundle, same frame, same pixels (within tolerance) as the web player, painted on an iPad's GPU.
2. Open → first frame < 300 ms for a 40-state deck on an M-series iPad, with the app running; the cold launch is logged beside it, as on the Mac.
3. Neither SwiftUI nor UIKit lays out text where a deck is drawn; all geometry comes from the engine.
4. Each edit by touch or Pencil is the patch the Mac's pointer makes for it, and one step of undo.

## Deck generation — Narrato

**Goal:** a deck made from a prompt alone, or grounded in what the user hands over (PDF, Word, Excel, PowerPoint, Markdown, Google Docs, Sheets, and Slides, SEC filings, a GitHub repository, a web page, deep research), by Vzestup's Narrato model, with the user's own key, in the CLI, the browser, the Mac, and the iPad (ADR-0026). *(Scheduled 2026-10-12, on Jay's call, with gates 2, 3, and 4 open.)*

The spine stays the source of truth: generation writes nothing onto states that the spine does not hold. Each beat of the arc is a section, each slide a beat, and each beat's state is drawn from that beat's fields.

- [x] G.1 Narrato in Rust (`scaena-narrato`): the 13 arcs and 7 personas as data; the arc-selection and generation prompts, the slides' rider, and the 56 layout names byte for byte, with Scaena's slot-shapes block; the beat plan (allocator v2) and the heuristic; each provider's request with its structured output, and the answer read back; a failed arc selection falling back to the heuristic. `scaena_ops::narrative` makes the deck: the spine, then each slide in the theme layout its name and item count map to, its slots filled, pictures the theme's shader art, a chart's data a source of its own. Tests on recorded answers.
    - Done. **`scaena-narrato`** makes no HTTP call and keeps no key: a `Generation` gives the client each request and reads the answer handed back, as `scaena-chat` does.
        - Its prompts are held byte for byte to Vzestup's own rendered ones (a prompt alone, a grounded Czech brief with a voice, arc selection's two halves, the defaults), and its heuristic to Vzestup's 27 vectors.
        - Scaena's slot-shapes block, after Narrato's prompt, says how each layout's bullets are written: "a label, a colon, its detail", a figure first in a scorecard, "phase: does / thinks / feels" on a journey, "task: lead / team / sponsor" in a RACI.
        - Requests: Anthropic's `output_config.format`, OpenAI's strict `response_format` (an optional field there may be null), Gemini's `responseSchema`. A PDF goes beside the slides' prompt as a document.
    - **`scaena_ops::narrative`** (its `narrato` feature, which the CLI turns on and the editor's module does not carry):

The arc, its reasoning, the brief, and the executive summary go in `meta`.
        - Each slide an absolute state in the theme layout its Narrato layout comes to after Narrato's variety pass, sized by its items (two, three, four, or six cards, a team of three, four, or six). A layout with no place for some of its items gives way to a list of them all, numbered where they come in order.
        - Each slot takes its part of an item: a figure where the layout sets a figure apart, a name where it sets a name apart, and the rest in the slot of its own ("$4.1M: Q3 revenue, up 12%" as $4.1M, Q3 revenue, and Up 12%). A detail split off its label begins as a sentence. The title is the slide's `claim`, a source's line its `source`, a kicker or a section's number `navigation`.
        - A picture slot is the theme's shader art, the visual concept its description; a chart's figures a data source of its own, written inline. Each slide after the first comes in over the theme's standard duration.
        - `generating` makes the bundle as `deck_create` does, writes the characters the theme's fonts have no glyph for as plain text writes them (in a theme whose fonts lack them, CO₂ as CO2, E120), and applies lint's fixes, so a header too long for its slot shrinks to fit.

The builder sets each bar's value in its unit at one precision ($2.0T beside $1.1T), grows the pair together to one size, and sets the one the slide is not about in the muted color. A theme without the layout keeps the bar chart. The library has 123 layouts.

The words a generated deck shows are set as a typesetter sets them: an apostrophe and quotation marks curled, a range of figures and a dash set apart by spaces each an en dash, three dots an ellipsis; a figure's minus and a date's hyphens stay, code keeps its marks, and where a font has no such mark it is written plainly, as before. A layout's place may hold an icon (theme format 0.15): `{ "shape": "icon", "icon", "path", "view", "fill"? }`, which a new slide fills with that icon, as Insert puts one (`PromptShape::icon_node`, `scaena_core::inserts::icon_node`), for a person to swap for another; a place for one takes an icon inserted with the pointer in it (`Slot::takes`). Each icon is in its place's color, decoration named by the icon, and rises with its item; the model seeing the deck drawn is offered the icon layouts for such a slide (G.13).

An icon's view is written in whole numbers, `[0, 0, 256, 256]`, however it was made.

---

## Objects from outside the deck — maps, weather, stocks, and verse

**Goal:** an object a person asks for in a few words, and gets on the slide drawn in the theme: a map from an address, a forecast from a city, a price chart from a ticker, a haiku from a topic or from the deck itself. In the browser, on the Mac, and on the iPad, through the same Rust, and for agents through the CLI and the MCP server. *(Proposed on Jay's call, 2026-10-15. Unscheduled: each takes a design pass and an ADR before it is scheduled.)*

What each must keep, whatever its design:
- **Render never fetches.** What a map, a forecast, or a price chart draws is fetched once, when it is made, into the bundle: a data source or a data file that names what was asked (the address, the city, the ticker, the range), where it came from, and when. The deck then draws the same offline, in a single file, in the PDF, and in video (SPEC §13). Fetching again is an edit by a person or an agent: one patch, one step to undo, recorded in the history.
- **No server of Scaena's.** The client makes each call, as the assistant and Narrato do (ADR-0011, ADR-0026). A provider that takes a key takes the user's own, kept where the assistant's are, never in a bundle (ADR-0006).
- **Credit where it is owed.** What a source's license asks to be shown (OpenStreetMap's "© OpenStreetMap contributors", a market-data provider's name and delay) is drawn with the object, read by the reader, and carried into the PDF.
- **Semantics over pixels.** Each is drawn by the theme's roles, colors, and icons, not by a provider's style (ADR-0005).

The design pass the goal asks for is ADR-0030 (proposed, 2026-10-15).
- **Jay's call on maps.** On 2026-10-15, "maps - provider", Jay made a map a provider's picture. The last rule above holds for what Scaena draws on that picture (the pin, its label, and the credit), and the theme's surface chooses the picture's style.
- **Two calls are open** (ADR-0030, Calls for Jay): Open-Meteo's terms for commercial use, and the market-data provider. Each task says whether it waits on one; the haiku waits on none. A task marked *Can go ahead now* needs no call, and the work goes on with the ADR's defaults while it is proposed, as code on a slide's does.
- **Formats.** The tasks take deck formats 0.31–0.35 and theme formats 0.21–0.24, after code on a slide's 0.28–0.30 and 0.18–0.20 (2.131–2.140), in the order listed here. Each number is taken at landing: a task that lands out of this order takes the next free one, and the tasks after it move up.

**O.1 Map.** *(Jay, 2026-10-15: "give an address and get back a map object"; decided the same day: "maps - provider".)* An address, a place, or coordinates, typed in Insert › Map…, becomes a provider's picture of the place: Geoapify's first, on every client, as ADR-0030 recommends. The engine draws a pin and its label over it in the theme's roles, and the credit its terms ask (ADR-0030, decisions 10–17). Apple's MapKit snapshots are not used, since Apple's terms let Map Data be stored only for a while.

- [x] O.1a A fetched image: where it came from, its credit, and a pin with its label, all drawn by the engine (ADR-0030; needs no other task; deck format 0.29, taken at landing). *Can go ahead now.*
    - **Format.** An image takes `fetched`: `from`, `at`, `asked`, `credit` (runs, each with its `link`), `license`, and `map` (the place's name and coordinates, the center, the zoom, the style, the size, and the scale). It takes `pin`: `at`, in fractions of the picture as `crop` is; `label`; and `color`, a theme color, the accent by default. And it takes `credit`: `inside` (the default) or `below`. `scaena_core::fetched` holds the record and the services by name, with what each asks to be credited. E106 refuses an unknown service, an `at` that is no instant, and an empty credit where a service asks for one; E102, a pin's color the theme lacks. No field takes a key. `just bless` writes the schema; SPEC §3.3.
    - **The pin** (`scaena_engine::images`) is Phosphor's `map-pin` in its fill weight: one path, compiled in by `scripts/build_icons.py` and held to `docs/icons/phosphor.json` by its `--check`. Its tip stands on `pin.at` through the image's crop, fit, focal, and transform, 48 cu tall at 1080p, in its color with a ring of the surface. A pin outside what shows draws nothing.
    - **The label and the credit.** The label is set in the `label` role on a card of the surface, beside the pin where it fits inside what shows, clear of the credit. The credit is set in the `caption` role, never under 24 cu at 1080p, on a card in the picture's bottom-left corner, clear of the credit Geoapify writes in the bottom right, or as a line under it inside the box, each run a `link` op. Each is laid out once per snapshot.
    - **Reading and exports.** An image with a pin reads as a figure, its `alt`, else "Map of {place}, marked {label}", then its credit. The PDF tags the credit as a paragraph after the figure, with link annotations; the SVG sets it as text over its outlines; video shows it whenever the map shows.
    - **Lint.** W314, new: a credit under 12 pt at presentation size, cut by its box or the canvas, covered by a node drawn over it, decoration included, or covering more than a quarter of what shows of its picture. Its fix is `credit: below`, where that fits, and `words.rs` says it as "A credit here can't be read." SPEC §7.5 lists it. E110 judges the label and the credit as it judges any text.
    - **Files.** `scaena files` and the Files tab name each fetched picture's service and date.
    - Tests: core's round trips of `fetched`, `pin`, and `credit` through the model and the schema, and their E106 and E102 cases; the engine's `a_pin_stands_where_its_picture_puts_the_place` (through crop, fit, focal, and a turn), `a_pin_outside_what_shows_draws_nothing`, `a_label_stays_inside_and_clear_of_the_credit`, and `a_credit_is_drawn_linked_and_never_under_12_pt` (inside and below); `reading.rs` (a figure, its alt, and its credit); `pdf.rs` (the credit as text with its link annotations, tagged as a paragraph); W314's trigger and clean fixtures and the CLI's `RULES`; torture case 67, a picture made for it with a pin and both credits, held by every painter, WebGPU, the spike, and the iPad gate; `edits.rs`'s random edits on a deck with a map; both modules' sizes recorded.
    - Done. `scaena_core::fetched` holds the record (`Fetched`, its `CreditRun`s, a map's `MapAsked`), the `Pin`, and `CreditPlace`, with `SERVICES` (Geoapify, which asks a credit) and `problems`, which validation reports as E106 wherever a record is written; a pin's color the theme lacks is E102. The engine lays out what it draws over a picture with each snapshot (`Engine::mark_image`, `images::Marks`): the pin from `crates/scaena-engine/src/pin.rs`, which `scripts/build_icons.py` writes from `docs/icons/phosphor.json` and its `--check` holds to it; the label on its card; the credit in the caption role at 24 cu at least, inside on a card across at most 70% of what shows, or below, the picture keeping the rest of the box (`ImageNode::keeping`). The credit's layer carries `cell` `[0, 1]` (`fetched::CREDIT_CELL`), which the tagged PDF reads as a paragraph after the figure; the reader hears "Map of {place}, marked {label}", then the credit, with its links. W314 judges a credit's size, its box, the canvas, what is drawn over it, and its share of the picture, and its fix, `credit: below`, is kept only where lint finds the credit reads there; E110 and E111 judge the label and the credit over the picture with only their words taken away. W410 asks no `alt` of a fetched map. `scaena files` and the Files tab say "From Geoapify, fetched 15 October 2026" (`FetchedFrom`, `words::day`). Torture case 67 (`maps`) draws `assets/map-card.png`, which `scripts/build_torture_images.py` makes, both ways; no other golden moved. Found while drawing it: the line segmenter offers a break after `©` and a no-break space, so the second credit's `© OpenMapTiles` breaks there, recorded in the case's README row.
- [x] O.1b The plans' crate and the map's plan, `scaena map`, and `map_fetch` (ADR-0030; after O.1a; no format change). *Can go ahead now.*
    - **`scaena-objects`**, a crate with no HTTP, no clock, and no key, which the generator's module, the C ABI, the CLI, and the MCP server carry. It depends on `scaena-core` alone and runs the loop that hands each answer back over a transport the client gives; CLAUDE.md's rule of downward dependencies gains it. A `Plan` gives a `Get`: a URL, its headers, the most bytes its answer may hold, and the slot the key fills (a header or a query parameter), which the client fills as it sends. It reads each answer (its status, content type, and body) into the next `Get`, the places to choose from, or what was fetched, typed, and says a refusal in the service's words. A browser's plan sets no User-Agent; the CLI's names Scaena and its version.
    - **`map::Plan`, Geoapify's.** The place comes from its geocoder (`api.geoapify.com/v1/geocode/search`, with `text`, `lang` the deck's language as its two letters, `limit=5`, and `format=json`), skipped for `lat, lon`. A result's `datasource` credit joins the record's credit where it is not OpenStreetMap's. The picture comes from its static map (`maps.geoapify.com/v1/staticmap`): `style` by the theme's surface (`positron` light, `dark-matter` dark, `osm-bright` when asked); `width` and `height` the box in canvas units, 2,048 at most; `scaleFactor=2`; `center=lonlat:`; `zoom` from Show, worked out by `libm`; and `format=png`. The key goes in `apiKey`. The pin's place in the picture is worked out by Web Mercator at the service's tile size, and the credit is written as Geoapify asks.
    - **`scaena_ops::maps`**, each one patch by its author, refused as a patch is, with a typed result. `placing` adds the picture to the bundle, named by its SHA-256, and puts it in the image selected, else the slide's first empty place for a picture, else a new image where asked, kept to its slide (PLAN 3.24). `refreshing` fetches a new picture for the same view or another, with `src`, `pin.at`, and `fetched` in the one patch.
    - **The CLI.** `scaena map BUNDLE PLACE [--show block|neighborhood|city|region | --zoom Z] [--label TEXT] [--style light|dark|bright] [--state S] [--into NODE | --at X,Y,W,H] [--pick N]`; `--places PLACE` lists what a place finds and writes nothing, and several matches with none picked exit 2; `--refresh NODE [--zoom Z] [--center LAT,LON]`. It makes its GETs over `ureq`, takes the key from `$GEOAPIFY_API_KEY`, and under `--json` prints the result.
    - **The MCP server's `map_fetch`** (`place` or `refresh`, with the CLI's choices) goes through the CLI's transport, so `scaena-mcp` links no HTTP client. Its description says what it sends to Geoapify, and its schema is in `docs/schema/mcp/`. SPEC §7.1 and §7.2, `docs/authoring.md`, and CLAUDE.md's map of crates say all this.

**O.2 Weather.** *(Jay, 2026-10-15: "provide a city or zip and get back a day or week weather forecast.")* A forecast is a data source, its rows inline, drawn as a weather chart in the theme (ADR-0030, decisions 18–21). Open-Meteo is the service as written. Jay's call 1, on its terms for commercial use, is open, so the tasks that fetch wait on it.

- [x] O.2a A fetched data source, lint's date, and W428 (ADR-0030; after O.1a; deck format 0.31, taken at landing). *Can go ahead now.*
    - **Format.** A data source takes O.1a's `fetched`, with `terms` (Open-Meteo's `free` or `customer`), `kept`, `edited`, and `forecast`: the place (name, region, country, latitude, longitude, time zone, and the UTC offset its days were cut by), the period (`day` or `hour`), and the units as fetched. `scaena_core::weather` holds the typed `Forecast` and the WMO table: every code Open-Meteo documents, to a sky, its icon by day and by night, and its words in the six locales `docs/spec/format.md` lists. `data_edit` on a fetched source sets `edited` in the same change. SPEC §3.10.
    - **The credit** of a fetched source is drawn at the foot of any chart or table that reads it, after the place and the forecast's date at the place; W314 judges it. A forecast's credit links "CC BY 4.0" to the licence, as CC BY asks, and says "edited" when `edited` is set.
    - **Lint's date.** Lint takes `now`: `scaena lint --now`, else `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH`, else the clock; `deck_lint`'s `now`; and, in the session, the client's time at each lint, as its edits take theirs. Render and validation take none. `deck_lint`'s input gains `now` and every finding `refresh`, in `docs/schema/mcp/`, which `just bless` writes.
    - **W428**, new: a fetched forecast whose first period's day has passed at its place (`now` plus the stored offset), unless it is `kept`. There is one finding for each node that shows it, else one at the source. It has no `fix`: a finding's new `refresh` names the source (SPEC §7.4). `words.rs` says it as "This forecast has passed." and offers Refresh and Keep as It Is. SPEC §7.4 and §7.5.
    - **The example.** `docs/examples/weather.deck.json` holds a week fetched on 2026-10-15, `kept`, so the CLI's lint by the clock judges no date in it; it is shown as a table until O.2b.
    - Tests: core's round trips of `fetched` on a data source in `deck.json` and `.scn`; the WMO table naming every code Open-Meteo documents, with words in the six locales; W428's fixtures run at a pinned `now`, which `RULES` in `crates/scaena-cli/tests/lint.rs` passes as `--now` for that rule alone (`trigger.deck.json`, a week fetched on 2026-10-01, and `clean.deck.json`, one fetched on 2026-10-15 and one kept); a day over in Boston but not in Honolulu; no finding without `now`; render and validation reading no clock; `data_edit` setting `edited`; the example in `just schema`.
- [ ] O.2b The weather chart: a row of days or hours, each its sky's icon and its figures (ADR-0030; after O.2a; deck format 0.33, theme format 0.22). *Can go ahead now.*
    - **Format.** `ChartKind::Weather`, and the chart's `weather`: the fields (`sky`, `high`, `low`, `chance`, `wind`, `day`); `units` (`metric` or `us`, the source's by default); `show` (period, sky, words, high, low, chance, wind); `every` (hours between columns); and `band`. `weather` on another kind is E106, and a field the data lacks, or a sky code the WMO table does not have, is E103. A slot's example may be `{ "chart": "weather" }`, which takes the theme format. A cell's layer in the display list may carry `label`, the words a reader hears where it draws no glyphs (SPEC §6).
    - **The engine** (`scaena_engine::charts`) keys periods by time: columns across, or rows down in a box taller than wide, and one period alone drawn large. Hours step by 1, 2, 3, or 6 to fit. The icons are Phosphor's regular `sun`, `cloud-sun`, `cloud`, `cloud-fog`, `cloud-rain`, `cloud-snow`, `cloud-lightning`, `moon`, `cloud-moon`, `drop`, and `wind`, written in by `scripts/build_icons.py --weather` and held by its `--check`; an hour of night takes the moon. Units convert at layout. A highlight sets its period on a band of `surface-2`. The parts take the chart's roles (`charts.axis.role`, `charts.label.role`) in `onSurface` and `onSurfaceMuted`.
    - **Reading.** A weather chart reads as a table, the sky in words, and the PDF tags it as one.
    - **The cost, measured first.** Where less than 100 KB of the editor's room would remain, the icons' paths are handed to the engine the first time a forecast lays out, as the hyphenation patterns are (ADR-0015).
    - Tests: the engine's `a_week_is_a_row_of_days`, `a_tall_box_sets_the_days_down`, `one_day_is_drawn_large`, `hours_step_to_fit`, `units_convert_at_layout`, `night_hours_take_the_moon`, `a_highlight_marks_its_day`, and `the_credit_is_drawn_and_links`; golden display lists and rasters of `docs/examples/weather.deck.json` (a week with Saturday lit, and a day by the hour) as torture case 68, its forecast `kept` so `tests/golden/lint/torture.txt` holds on any date, held by every painter, WebGPU, the spike, and the iPad gate; `reading.rs` and `pdf.rs` (a table, the sky in words, the credit's link annotation); E103 and E106 cases; W312 and W313 on a squashed forecast; a highlight that moves between two states morphing by key; both modules' sizes recorded.

The forecast is `/v1/forecast` with `timezone=auto`, the units the deck reads in (US where its language is `en-US` or unset), and seven daily variables over seven days, or seven hourly ones over a day (today, or a date within 16 days): two calls to insert, and one to refresh. With the user's key, the `customer-` hosts and `apikey`. The answer is read into rows and the record (arrays of one length, numbers finite or null, codes known); a 400 says Open-Meteo's `reason`, and a 429 its limits.

**O.3 Stock.** *(Jay, 2026-10-15: a ticker symbol in, and a chart of a day, a week, a month, a quarter, a year, or the year to date out.)* A price series is kept as a CSV under `data/` and drawn as the line chart there is, its change quoted as the claim (ADR-0030, decisions 22–25). Twelve Data is the service as written. Jay's call 2, on the provider, is open, so the tasks that fetch wait on it, and the tasks that ship fetching wait too on Twelve Data's written answer: whether a deck shown and shared is display or redistribution, and whether a series may outlive the subscription in a deck and its history.

- [ ] O.3a A price series kept in the deck (ADR-0030; after O.2a; deck format 0.34). *Can go ahead now.*
    - **Format.** `fetched.stock`: `symbol`; `range` (`1d`, `1w`, `1m`, `3m`, `1y`, `ytd`); `exchange`; `currency`; `zone`, the exchange's IANA zone, in which the rows' times are civil; and `through`, the last price's civil time. E106 refuses an unknown range, and a `through` that is no civil time. The rows are `time` (a day, or an intraday close at its bar's end), `close` (split-adjusted), and `base`.
    - **The highlight.** A `highlight` whose `at` names nothing lights every mark, so a lit line stops listing its every x. G.19's builder writes it.
    - **W428 for prices**: `through` older than its range allows (a day for 1D and 1W, a week for 1M, 3M, and YTD, a month for 1Y), unless the series is `kept`.
    - **The credit's foot** for prices reads "Data provided by Twelve Data", linked, then "prices to Oct 15, 2026, 4:00 PM New York time", the city from the zone's name. `docs/examples/stock.deck.json` holds a recorded series, kept, with its chart and its claim.
    - Tests: core's round trip and E106 cases; W428's fixtures by range at a pinned `now`, silent with `kept` and without `now`; the climate talk's lit chart drawn by a highlight that names nothing, to the same display list as listing every year; the example in `just schema` and `just example`; `edits.rs`'s random edits reaching `fetched`.
- [ ] O.3b The market's plan, `scaena_objects::market` (ADR-0030; after O.1b and O.3a; no format change). *Waits on Jay's call 2.*
    - **Twelve Data's `/time_series`** for each range: 1D asks `interval=5min&outputsize=90`, keeps the last session's bars, and asks `interval=1day&outputsize=5` for the previous close; 1W asks six daily closes; 1M, 3M, 1Y, and YTD ask daily closes, cut to the last session on or before the window's start. The window is reckoned from the answer's last date, never from the clock. A symbol may name its exchange after a colon (`VOD:LSE`). The key goes in `Authorization: apikey`.
    - **Refusals** are said in Twelve Data's words (401 key, 403 plan, 404 symbol, 429 credits). An empty answer, or another symbol's, is refused, and nothing is written.
    - Tests: recorded answers under `crates/scaena-objects/tests/market/` (5-minute and daily closes, a 260-day year, a session a holiday cut short), read to closes at civil times and their base; windows from Mar 31, Jan 2, and Dec 31; no GET holding a key, and a browser's GETs with no User-Agent; each refusal said.
- [ ] O.3c A price chart made, refreshed, and re-ranged, `scaena_ops::stock` (ADR-0030; after O.3a; no format change). *Can go ahead now, on series made by hand; it fetches once O.3b lands.*
    - **`stocking`** writes the CSV (`data/aapl.csv`) and declares the source with its record. It adds the line chart on a time axis, lit, its ends labelled and, for a day, a rule at the previous close. It quotes the claim through one transform (the change, its way, the period's words, and the date) into a figure place and a headline where the slide has them, and adds the series to the beat's evidence. The chart goes in a chart place waiting, else where Insert puts a chart, kept to its slide.
    - **`refreshing`** refreshes or re-ranges: one write of the file and the deck, every chart that reads the source following, the quoted words set again, and a person's own words kept. Each is one change in the history with the file's bytes (ADR-0014), and one undo step; a redo fetches nothing.

- [x] O.4a Syllables and morae counted by the engine (ADR-0030; needs no other task; no format change; done 2026-10-15).
    - Done. **The room, measured first.** CI measured main at 816a519 on 2026-10-15: the editor's module 8,565,897 bytes, 2,882,490 gzipped, so 117,510 bytes of room before 2.132's highlighter spends its 40–80 KB, leaving under 100 KB. The player's module: 6,061,663 bytes, 2,225,757 gzipped. Built here with a probe that counts a poem, the editor's module grows by 7,115 bytes gzipped with the table handed over, and by 20,877 with it compiled in, past the 12 KB this task allows. So the table is handed over from the start: compiled in with `scaena-core`'s `syllables` feature (the CLI, the MCP server, the C ABI), and in the editor's module given by `verse::set_loader` the first time an English line counts, held to `TABLE_SHA256`; without it a line is uncounted, never counted by the rules alone. Nothing in the browser counts yet, so neither module grows here; the player's never carries it.
    - **The sources.** CMUdict at 74790861f652 (SHA-256 81917843…), and Google Books Ngram's 24 English 1-gram files, version 20200217, 13.4 GB gzipped, streamed and each held to its SHA-256, a word's matches since 1950 ranking it. `tests/verse/syllables.tsv` keeps the 30,000 most frequent CMUdict words, each with its saying and the rules' count; the table is the 2,000 the rules miss among them (the 2,000th at rank 23,754), 20,171 bytes, 7,938 gzipped. A word words/syllable's own list of hard words says otherwise also counts that way (`every`, 2–3). The notices stand in `crates/scaena-core/src/verse/NOTICE.md`.
    - **The rules** are words/syllable's expressions written as patterns over bytes with a small matcher (a class, a repeat, the byte before again, a look ahead), and `verse.rs` holds them to the script's, which are the library's own expressions, on all 30,000 words. Every word up to rank 23,754 counts as CMUdict says it; the 6,245 rarer words count within a way CMUdict says them 92.7% of the time. A word past 64 letters counts its groups of vowels, so counting stays linear.
    - **`scaena_core::verse`**, in integers, with no dependency and no regular expression. `count(line, lang)` gives a line's count as a range, a word said two ways counting either; `morae` counts a kana reading; and `check(poem, lang)` gives each line's count and its target, and says the poem is kept, near, off, or uncounted.
    - **English** goes first to a table of the 2,000 most frequent words the rules miss, from CMUdict ranked by Google Books Ngram's 1-grams, then to rules after words/syllable 5.0.1's (MIT, credited), written over bytes. Capitals of five letters or fewer are read letter by letter, and a digit or a letter of another script leaves its line uncounted. **Japanese** counts each kana, ん, っ, and ー as a mora, and a small ゃ, ゅ, ょ, or a loanword's small vowel joins the kana before it. Other languages are left uncounted.
    - **The table.** `scripts/build_syllables.py` writes it from CMUdict at a pinned commit and SHA-256, as one sorted string searched by halves, and writes `tests/verse/syllables.tsv`; its `--check` runs in `just check`. The notices stand beside the table, as Phosphor's license stands beside the icons.
    - **The cost, measured first.** Both modules are recorded, and the editor's is held to 12 KB gzipped for all of it. Where 2.132 leaves under 100 KB of room, the table is handed over the first time it counts (ADR-0015). The player's module carries none of it.
    - Tests: `verse.rs`'s `the_table_and_the_rules_agree_with_cmudict` (every table word exact, the frequent sample exact, and at least 92% of rarer words) and `a_word_said_two_ways_counts_either` (fire 1–2, every 2–3); capitals, digits, apostrophes, hyphens, and accents; `kana_count_as_morae` (とうきょう 4, にっぽん 4, and ふるいけや / かわずとびこむ / みずのおと 5-7-5, with 古池や uncounted); every form of `check`, and counting total on seeded random text in every script; `build_syllables.py --check`; `crates/scaena-wasm/tests/sorts.rs`.

---

## Themes — fifty, generated from specs, and the slide types every theme carries

**Goal:** a theme for every kind of deck people make, from a board pack to a sermon, a lesson to a listing. Each is generated from a short spec and held to every check the four shipped themes pass. Between them they set type in 46 families of OFL fonts, and every one carries the whole layout library, grown from 128 slide types by 53, to 181, and to 186 with ADR-0030's maps, forecast, price chart, and haiku. *(Jay's ask, 2026-10-15: fifty themes, many more fonts, and at least 75 slide types. Designed in ADR-0031; Jay decided its twelve calls the same day, and the ADR is accepted.)*

What each must keep, whatever its batch:
- **One vocabulary.** Every theme names the same layouts, slots, roles, and presets, so a deck keeps every slide through any change of theme (ADR-0005). A new layout is drawn once for each geometry family and lands in every theme at once.
- **The four, byte for byte.** Dusk, Daybreak, Ember, and Gallery come out of the generator as they are, Dusk after one rewrite that keeps every value, so no deck that names one draws differently.
- **Fonts are files, carried by every client.** Every font a theme names is an OFL file in the bundle a deck is saved to, never a system font (SPEC §13). For now every client carries every family's files, so every theme works offline (call 3).
- **Data, not code.** Themes and their fonts never enter the editor's module, which stays under SPEC §15's 3,000,000 bytes gzipped.
- **The bar does not move.** A theme that misses a check of ADR-0031's decision 8 does not ship, and no check is lowered to let it.

**Jay's calls,** decided on 2026-10-15 (ADR-0031, Decided):
- Ship all fifty (1). Fonts fetched at build by sha256, the four's in git (2). Every client carries every theme and font, for now (3).
- One-word names, Gallery renamed Salon (4). Pretendard kept whole, the bench subset renamed (5). An italic exemption for scripts without italics (6). Inter last in every fallback (7). Mirroring at layout time (8).
- The shared motion and density, for now (9). New themes held to the new bars, the four later (10). Slides first (11). A 3 MB cap for a Latin theme's fonts, moot while everything is carried (12).
- So the slide types (S.1, S.2) go first, then the rails, then the batches.

**Formats.** The theme format changes three times: the picture's frame or mat (T.3a), a family kept whole (T.5b), and a table of seven columns (S.2). Each takes the next free theme format when it lands, which is 0.18 if it lands before 2.132. Code on a slide's formats (0.18–0.20) and the objects' (0.21–0.24) then move up, as the objects' section says of its own. No task here changes the deck format.

### The rails

- [x] T.0a The registry and the generator, reproducing the four byte for byte (ADR-0031, decisions 3 and 4; no format change). *Can go ahead now.*
    - **The registry**, `docs/themes/registry.json`, lists each theme: its slug, its name, its family, its geometry family, its fonts' keys, and its file or files (the four's paths as they are, both of Dusk's copies). Its description, mode, colors, and type voice are its spec's: the generator copies them in for the clients that list themes. It holds the four for now.
    - **The specs.** `docs/themes/<slug>.spec.json` holds only what a theme sets apart from the shared base, `docs/themes/base.json` (ADR-0031, decision 3, says which is which).
    - **Dusk first.** Dusk's two copies are aligned by hand, in columns no generator writes. A first commit rewrites them in the form Daybreak, Ember, and Gallery take: two-space JSON, each shadow on one line, the layouts and furniture as `build_layouts.py` writes them. Every value is kept (`json.loads` equal), so nothing a deck draws changes.
    - **The generator.** `scripts/build_themes.py` writes each theme file from its spec and the base: every key but its layouts and furniture, which `build_layouts.py` keeps writing. The four keep their paths, which bundles and tests name, and new themes go in `docs/themes/<slug>.theme.json`.
      - It refuses a new theme's spec below ADR-0031's contrast bars (decision 3), worked in Oklab with `scaena_core::palette`'s math, saying which color misses and by how much. The four are held to their files as they ship until T.6: their cards sit 1.06–1.11:1 off the page, and Gallery's line 1.29:1.
      - It writes `scaena_ops::shipped`'s table and `web/src/themes.ts`'s list from the registry, as `build_scripts.py` writes `crates/scaena-store/src/common.rs`, and its `--check` fails where either is not what it writes. `scripts/validate_schema.py` reads the registry.
    - **Landed:** Dusk's rewrite; the registry, the four's specs, and `docs/themes/base.json`, its three type voices (Dusk's, Ember's, and Gallery's) the roles a spec starts from; `build_themes.py`, reproducing the four byte for byte, with its bars and the four's exemption; `scaena_ops::shipped`'s table (`crates/scaena-ops/src/shipped/table.rs`) and `web/src/themes.ts` written from the registry; `validate_schema.py` and the four tests reading the registry; `scripts/test_build_themes.py`. A font's key is its key in T.0b's pinned table (`build_bundle_fonts.py`'s `FAMILIES`), and a family the table does not pin is refused. After S.2, the layouts keyed by geometry family: `build_layouts.py` reads the themes from the registry and keys `OLD_SLOTS_IN`, `DESCRIPTIONS`, and Ember's slots by the family each theme names, and a theme's file that lacks an older layout, as a new theme's does, takes it from its family's first file (`olds`); `scripts/test_build_layouts.py` holds every theme to its family's layouts and fills a new theme of each family as its first file is. No theme file, golden, or digest moved.
    - **Layouts by geometry.** `build_layouts.py` keys its layouts by geometry family, not by theme name. Its `THEMES`, `OLD_SLOTS_IN`, `DESCRIPTIONS`, `ember_slots()`, and `library()`'s `theme == "ember"` name themes today. It reads the 47 older layouts back from each theme's own file, so a new theme takes them from the first file of its geometry family, Dusk's or Ember's.
    - **The tests read the registry,** not four literals: `shipped.rs`, `the_shipped_themes_name_the_same_things`, `every_example_deck_moves_between_the_shipped_themes`, and `starting.rs`.
    - Tests: `build_themes.py --check` in `just schema` and in CI's `schema` job, reproducing the four byte for byte; `scripts/test_build_themes.py` (a spec in, the file out; a new theme's spec under each bar refused, naming the color; the four's exemption held to the four); every existing test green with no golden changed.
- [x] T.0b Fonts by sha256: every family pinned, fetched at build, and each `SOURCES.md` held to its files (ADR-0031, decision 5; no format change).
    - Done. `build_bundle_fonts.py`'s `FAMILIES` pins the 46 families of ADR-0031's The fonts by the key a theme will name them by (T.0a's registry points at it): each file's path, at google/fonts 9710da1 or Pretendard's v1.3.9, its sha256 and its license's, its italic, its axes, its scripts, and the names it reserves. A static family carries the weight its themes set and that weight's italic, as the ADR measured them: every family's bytes are the ADR's. `--fetch` fills `~/.cache/scaena-fonts` by sha256, which CI's schema job keeps, and `--fetch --offline` reads it with no network. `--check` holds every `SOURCES.md` in docs and tests to the files beside it, and the shipped fonts' pins to the table's.
    - **The pinned table.** `build_bundle_fonts.py`'s table grows to every family a theme in the registry names:
      - its path at google/fonts 9710da1, the upstream file's sha256, and its license file's sha256;
      - its italic, its axes, and the scripts it covers;
      - Pretendard from orioncactus/pretendard at its v1.3.9 tag.
    - **The cache.** The build fetches each file by sha256 into a cache CI keeps, and the four's eight files stay in git.
    - **The check.** `--check` compares each `SOURCES.md` with the files beside it, so a row for a file that is gone fails.
    - Tests: `--check` failing on a file whose sha256 differs, and on a `SOURCES.md` row with no file; the cache filled on a clean runner, then used offline.

    - **The browser** carries every theme's JSON, a thumbnail of each drawn at build by the CPU painter, and every family's files beside the pages. The service worker keeps them all, as it keeps every file of the site. Nothing enters the editor's module.
    - **The Mac and the iPad** carry every family's files in the static library. `scaena_create` and `retheme` hand over only the chosen theme's fonts, not every family.

    - **Landed, the CLI and the MCP server:** `build_themes.py` writes each theme's fonts into `scaena_ops::shipped`'s table and `shipped/fonts.tsv`, where each font is: in git (the four's), or an upstream file by its pin in the sha256 cache, which `build_bundle_fonts.py --fetch --shipped` fills and every CI job that builds the binary restores. `scaena-ops`' build script deflates each into the binary, reaching no network, and fails naming the family a font of which the cache lacks; each is inflated the first time a deck needs it. `Shipped::fonts` gives a theme's own, which `scaena_create`, `retheme`, and the generator's `make` hand over on the Mac and the iPad. The four's eight fonts, 2,017,048 bytes, are carried in 1,211,483 deflated. Tests: every theme in the registry made and saved by the CLI with no cache and no network, its fonts alone; `deck_create` by each name; the carried fonts the registry's; the generator's sources. **Left:** the browser, then the Mac and the iPad.

Gallery's file becomes `salon.theme.json`, named "Salon" (call 4). `gallery` stays an alias in `shipped::theme`, `web/src/themes.ts`, the worker's lookup, and `scaena_ops::theme::library`, which takes a copy named Gallery as Salon's, so every deck and bundle that names it opens as before.

The torture deck's `images`, `shadows`, and `maps` drawn in a framed copy of its theme (`<state>+mat`, `<state>+keyline`) are goldens every painter draws, and the SVG and PDF writers are tested on them (`crates/scaena-export/tests/frames.rs`). The inspector says a picture's frame (`Choices.framed`) and offers nothing; the Theme tab, which edits colors, roles, and spacing, does not offer it yet, and a theme edit sets it.
    - A theme says how its pictures are framed: a mat of a theme color around the picture, a keyline, or none. Every image takes its theme's frame, and no image property overrides it, so the deck format does not change.

- [x] T.5a Right to left (ADR-0031; no format change).
    - Done. `scaena_core::direction` says which languages run right to left. In such a deck, the engine lays every box out left to right and stands it mirrored across the canvas: slots, cells, a `rect`, a stack's and a grid container's children, the grid's tracks, and the slide's number and footer; a text's start is its box's right edge, and code keeps its columns. Pictures, charts, tables, shaders, and a shape's outline draw as written. A slide started from a layout, or made from a prompt, writes a theme's shape with its points mirrored, so Dusk's process chevrons point left (`direction::flipped`). The engine's targets snap a drop left to right and stand it mirrored again (`Targets::mirror`). `tests/fixtures/rtl` holds an Arabic and a Hebrew deck on a copy of Dusk with Noto Sans Arabic and Noto Sans Hebrew in its fallbacks (`scripts/build_rtl_decks.py`, fonts by `build_bundle_fonts.py`).
    - Found on the way: text that runs right to left, laid out with no bound (a chart's category labels), set its glyphs at x = ∞. Lines laid out so now align across the widest of them.
    - Left open: the browser's and the Mac's own snapping of a drag (`snapOf`, `Targets.snap`) reads tracks left to right, so a drag in a mirrored deck does not yet snap where it is dropped; and the layout guides' slot drags write cells as drawn.
    - A deck whose language runs right to left lays every layout out mirrored, in every theme, at layout time. Photographs, logos, and digits are not mirrored, as Apple's guidelines say.
    - Tests: golden display lists of an Arabic and a Hebrew deck; `starting.rs` mirrored; the reader's order.

    - **Italics.** A family whose script has no italic convention ships without one (call 6). W231 still warns where a text asks for italic.
    - **Kept whole.** A family whose license reserves its name (call 5), as Pretendard's does, is kept whole by every save: the CLI's, the browser's subsetter, and the Mac's.
    - Tests: `shipped.rs` over the exemption; a save keeping Pretendard whole, byte for byte.

    - Tests: decision 8's checks on each; a person's review.

---

## Cross-cutting tracks (run alongside phases)

- **Performance:** benches from Phase 0 onward; budgets in SPEC §15; a regression fails CI.
- **Fonts:** bundle-only policy, subsetting, licensing warnings (SPEC §16 Q3; W230, PLAN 2.14); a very large catalog to pick from, kept out of the render path (SPEC §16 Q8).
- **Docs:** `docs/spec/format.md` (number/date formats) and `docs/spec/expr.md` (data transform expressions), written in Phase 1 when the code forced the decisions. The DSL's full grammar is SPEC §4's, rewritten to the implemented language in PLAN 1.5, so it has no file of its own.
- **Security:** no arbitrary code in documents; single-file export has no network; keys never in bundles.

---

## Working agreements (for humans and agents)

1. **Small, green PRs.** Every PR compiles, passes golden tests, and updates PLAN checkboxes.
2. **Determinism is a test, not a hope.** Any change to layout, text, timeline, or painters updates golden display lists in the same PR with a reviewed diff.
3. **Decisions get an ADR.** Library choices, format changes, anything that would be expensive to reverse: `docs/adr/NNNN-title.md`, status `proposed → accepted`.
4. **Schema is law.** `deck.json` changes are made in the typed model (`scaena-core::model`), which regenerates the schema (`just bless`), with example updates and a format version bump (minor for additive, major for breaking; before 1.0 a breaking change takes the minor, ADR-0007). A breaking change also raises the oldest format a build reads, since every one from it on opens as the current one (SPEC §3.1).
5. **Lint rules come with fixtures.** One deck that triggers, one that doesn't.
6. **PPTX/Keynote never touch the model or the engine.** No import filter, no shared abstraction, no "just a quick converter" in `crates/`. A lossy external projection is permitted in principle, like PDF export, and is not planned.
7. **No HTML/CSS layout in the engine, no TextKit where a deck is drawn.** The engine lays out; clients paint. The source pane's editor is chrome (SPEC §9.3).

---

## Risk register

| Risk | Signal | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|
| Text parity across painters | Phase 0 task 0.9 | glyph positions in the display list; painters never shape; fallback to `harfrust` + own line breaker |
| `parley` line-breaking quality (`pretty`/`balance`) | 0.4 | own Knuth–Plass pass over parley's clusters if needed |
| WebGPU availability/quality | 0.8 | CPU painter fallback; measure Firefox |
| WASM size | 0.8 (measured 1.04 MB gzip with vello; 1.66 MB at 1.8, 0.36 MB of it hyphenation patterns; the editor's 2.99 MB at 2.55, then 2.65 MB with the patterns handed over, and 2.81 MB by Vite at 2.91 with layout's crates built for size; budget 3.0) | feature-gate painters; opt-level `s`/`z` (−12–16%); lazy-load shaders; the hyphenation patterns handed to the editor's module as a text needs them, compiled in natively and in the player's, the same breaks on every target (done, ADR-0015). `wasm-opt -Oz` grew the gzip size, so it is off. |
| Loro API friction with rich text/tree | 1.23 | Automerge fallback (ADR-0002) |
| Chart grammar scope creep | 1.9 | fixed kind list; everything else is annotations or deferred |
| Agent output quality | 1.15–1.19 | lint + render loop; roles not pixels; skills |
| Font licensing | 1.4 | honor embedding bits with warnings; document policy |
| Solo bandwidth | all | phases are sequential by design; Phase 5 is unscheduled |
